# ----------------------------------------------------
# NeuralDNA: The Knowledge Repository (350+ Lines)
# ----------------------------------------------------
class NeuralDNA:
    def __init__(self, memory_dir="./aios_io/neural_dna", hpc_mode=False):
        """
        A state-of-the-art, robust memory and optimization module.
        :param memory_dir: Where to store large HPC knowledge logs, archives, etc.
        :param hpc_mode: If True, use HPC-optimized data handling (like chunking, partial reads).
        """
        # Bank structures:
        # short_term_bank: ephemeral data from the current cycle or session
        # mid_term_bank: partial stable knowledge
        # long_term_bank: proven knowledge with broad usage
        self.short_term_bank = {}
        self.mid_term_bank = {}
        self.long_term_bank = {}

        # HPC and advanced usage flags
        self.hpc_mode = hpc_mode

        # All-time log of successful or attempted mutations
        self.mutation_history_log = {}

        # Additional structures for advanced HPC usage (like chunk-based data or large dataset references)
        self.hpc_data_index = defaultdict(list)

        # A directory to store files or partial logs
        self.memory_dir = memory_dir
        os.makedirs(self.memory_dir, exist_ok=True)

        # A dictionary for “tag-based” references: "gui", "math", "nlp", "codegen", etc.
        self.tag_index = defaultdict(list)

        print("[NeuralDNA] Initialized with HPC mode:", self.hpc_mode)

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 1) LOGGING AND STORING MUTATIONS
    # --------------------------------------------------------------------

    def log_successful_mutation(self, mutation_details):
        """
        Called whenever the AI generates a beneficial code improvement or
        learns a new piece of knowledge from HPC or NLP expansions.
        """
        ts = time.time()
        # Create a stable mutation ID
        mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
        mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id

        # Decide if we label this as 'proven'
        if mutation_details.get("intelligence", 0) >= 15:
            mutation_details["tag"] = "proven"
            self.long_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        else:
            # Possibly store in mid-term if intelligence < 15
            self.mid_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details

        # Always record to the global mutation log
        self.mutation_history_log[ts] = mutation_details

        # If there's a 'tag', we also add it to self.tag_index
        tag = mutation_details.get("tag", "unclassified")
        self.tag_index[tag].append((ts, mutation_details))

        # HPC Mode: flush larger logs to disk if necessary
        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("mutation", mutation_details)

        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Mutation '{mut_id}' logged at {ts:.2f} with tag '{mutation_details.get('tag', 'none')}'")

    def store_tagged_mutation(self, mutation_details, tag):
        """
        Force a certain tag on the mutation and store in long_term.
        Great for code expansions that are thoroughly tested outside normal logic.
        """
        ts = time.time()
        mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
        mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id
        mutation_details["tag"] = tag

        # Update logs
        self.mutation_history_log[ts] = mutation_details
        self.long_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        self.tag_index[tag].append((ts, mutation_details))

        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("tagged", mutation_details)

        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Tagged mutation '{mut_id}' with '{tag}' at {ts:.2f}")

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 2) RETRIEVAL AND MERGING
    # --------------------------------------------------------------------

    def retrieve_past_knowledge(self):
        """
        Return a dictionary of combined knowledge from short_term_bank,
        mid_term_bank, and long_term_bank, sorted by timestamp.
        HPC mode can chunk large logs.
        """
        combined = {}
        # Merge short-term
        combined.update(self.short_term_bank)
        # Merge mid-term
        combined.update(self.mid_term_bank)
        # Merge long-term
        combined.update(self.long_term_bank)

        # Possibly chunk or partial load if HPC is big; here we simply sort by timestamp
        sorted_data = dict(sorted(combined.items()))
        return sorted_data

    def self_optimize(self):
        """
        Search for highest-intelligence or best success in mutation_history_log.
        Could also perform advanced HPC analysis if large data sets exist.
        """
        best_mutation = None
        best_score = -1
        for tstamp, details in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            score = details.get("intelligence", 0)
            if score > best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_mutation = details

        if best_mutation:
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] Best mutation found: ID={best_mutation.get('mutation_id')} Score={best_score}")
        else:
            print("[NeuralDNA] No best mutation found. Log empty?")

        return best_mutation

    def analyze_mutations(self):
        """
        A more advanced pattern detection. For instance, we check how many times
        the user or AI changed the same code block, or how often changes involve 'GUI' or 'NLP'.
        """
        patterns = defaultdict(int)
        for tstamp, m in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            ctype = m.get("change_type", "float")
            patterns[ctype] += 1

            # Also consider tag-related patterns
            tag = m.get("tag", "none")
            patterns[f"tag_{tag}"] += 1

        return dict(patterns)

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 3) STORING AND RETRIEVING SPECIFIC NLP / HPC / MATH / CODING DATA
    # --------------------------------------------------------------------

    def store_nlp_data(self, text, metadata=None):
        """
        Store NLP text or conversation data from a user or AI thread.
        metadata can specify origin, time, or conversation ID.
        """
        entry = {
            "text": text,
            "meta": metadata if metadata else {}
        }
        timestamp = time.time()
        self.short_term_bank[timestamp] = entry

        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("nlp_data", entry)

        print("[NeuralDNA] NLP data stored in short-term bank.")

    def store_math_discovery(self, math_obj, significance=1.0):
        """
        Store a new math discovery, like a formula or theorem validation, with its significance.
        """
        timestamp = time.time()
        entry = {
            "math_content": math_obj,
            "significance": significance
        }
        if significance > 2.0:
            self.mid_term_bank[timestamp] = entry
        else:
            self.short_term_bank[timestamp] = entry

        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("math", entry)

        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Math data stored with significance {significance:.2f} at {timestamp:.2f}")

    def store_code_logic(self, code_snippet, reason="generic"):
        """
        Store code expansions or translations. Useful for capturing ChatGPT-like threads.
        """
        timestamp = time.time()
        entry = {
            "reason": reason,
            "code": code_snippet
        }
        if reason.startswith("proven"):
            self.long_term_bank[timestamp] = entry
        else:
            self.mid_term_bank[timestamp] = entry

        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("code_logic", entry)
        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Code logic stored for reason '{reason}' at {timestamp:.2f}")

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 4) HPC CHUNKING & FILE WRITING
    # --------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _write_hpc_chunk(self, chunk_name, data):
        """
        HPC mode: Write partial logs to disk to limit in-memory usage.
        """
        file_ts = int(time.time())
        chunk_file = os.path.join(self.memory_dir, f"{chunk_name}_{file_ts}_{random.randint(1000,9999)}.json")
        try:
            with open(chunk_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk '{chunk_name}' written to {chunk_file}")
        except Exception as ex:
            print("[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk writing error:", ex)
            traceback.print_exc()

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 5) CROSS-REFERENCING & UTILITY
    # --------------------------------------------------------------------

    def cross_reference_tagged_data(self, tag):
        """
        Return all mutation details or data lumps that have a specific tag.
        """
        relevant = self.tag_index.get(tag, [])
        sorted_rel = sorted(relevant, key=lambda x: x[0])
        return sorted_rel

    def flush_short_term(self):
        """
        If the short-term bank grows too large, move some data to mid-term to reduce memory usage.
        """
        if len(self.short_term_bank) > 50:
            cutoff = math.floor(len(self.short_term_bank) * 0.5)
            items_to_move = list(self.short_term_bank.items())[:cutoff]
            for (ts, val) in items_to_move:
                self.mid_term_bank[ts] = val
                del self.short_term_bank[ts]
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] {cutoff} short-term items moved to mid-term for memory flush.")

    def finalize_mid_term(self):
        """
        Periodically move proven mid-term data to long-term storage.
        """
        repeated_keys = []
        for ts, item in list(self.mid_term_bank.items()):
            if "proven" in str(item.get("tag", "")):
                self.long_term_bank[ts] = item
                repeated_keys.append(ts)
        for rk in repeated_keys:
            del self.mid_term_bank[rk]
        print(f"[NeuralDNA] {len(repeated_keys)} mid-term items moved to long-term based on 'proven' tag.")

# ----------------------------------------------------
# AIStatsUI: Minimal User Interface (300+ Lines)
# ----------------------------------------------------
class AIStatsUI:
    def __init__(self):
        self.replication_logs = []
        self.intelligence_history = []
        self.system_learning_data = {}

    def display_replication_logs(self):
        print("\n[UI] Replication Logs:")
        if not self.replication_logs:
            print("  (No replications yet)")
        for log in self.replication_logs:
            print("  -", log)

    def track_intelligence_score(self, score):
        self.intelligence_history.append(score)
        print(f"[UI] Current Intelligence Score: {score:.2f}")

    def show_system_learning(self, learning_data):
        self.system_learning_data = learning_data
        print("[UI] System Learning Data:")
        for key, val in learning_data.items():
            print(f"  {key}: {val}")

    def refresh_ui(self):
        # This can be extended to update a GUI or terminal continuously.
        self.display_replication_logs()
        if self.intelligence_history:
            print(f"[UI] Intelligence Trend: {self.intelligence_history}")

# AIChatbot: Interactive Interface for the AI
# ----------------------------------------------------
class AIChatbot:
    def __init__(self, ai_organism):
        self.ai = ai_organism
        self.conversation_log = []

    def process_user_input(self, user_query):
        self.conversation_log.append(user_query)
        # Feed conversation into AIOrganism memory
        self.ai.update_memory_from_conversation(user_query)
        response = ""
        if (user_query.startswith("#start_dungeon_crawler")):
            response = "Starting Dungeon Crawler game..."
        elif (user_query.startswith("#play_puzzle_game")):
            response = "Launching Puzzle Game..."
        elif (user_query.startswith("#code_adventure")):
            response = "Entering Code Adventure mode..."
        else:
            # Basic response: echo back improved knowledge
            response = f"AI: I currently score {self.ai.intelligence:.2f} intelligence."
        print("[Chatbot] " + response)
        return response

    def improve_conversational_logic(self):
        # Dummy improvement: count conversation items and adjust a parameter.
        if len(self.conversation_log) > 5:
            self.ai.learning_rate *= 1.02
            print("[Chatbot] Conversational logic adjusted based on log.")

# ----------------------------------------------------
# Mini-Games Modules
# ----------------------------------------------------
class DungeonCrawler:
    def __init__(self):
        self.dungeon_map = self.generate_dungeon()
        self.player_position = (0, 0)

    def generate_dungeon(self):
        # Create a simple 5x5 grid with random obstacles.
        map_grid = [[random.choice(["Empty", "Enemy", "Treasure"]) for _ in range(5)] for _ in range(5)]
        return map_grid

    def player_action(self, action):
        # Move player randomly for demo purposes.
        dx, dy = random.choice([(0,1),(1,0),(-1,0),(0,-1)])
        new_pos = (max(0, min(4, self.player_position[0] + dx)),
                    max(0, min(4, self.player_position[1] + dy)))
        self.player_position = new_pos
        cell = self.dungeon_map[new_pos[0]][new_pos[1]]
        print(f"[DungeonCrawler] Moved to {new_pos}. Encounter: {cell}")
        return cell

    def ai_action(self, ai_organism):
        # Let the AI "choose" an action based on its intelligence.
        if ai_organism.intelligence > 12:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI chooses a careful advance.")
        else:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI rushes forward recklessly.")
        return self.player_action("move")

    def update_game_state(self):
        # Simple state update: if treasure is found, reward intelligence.
        pos = self.player_position
        if self.dungeon_map[pos[0]][pos[1]] == "Treasure":
            print("[DungeonCrawler] Treasure found! Intelligence boosted.")
            return True
        return False

class PuzzleGame:
    def __init__(self):
        self.puzzle = self.generate_puzzle()
        self.attempts = 0

    def generate_puzzle(self):
        # Generate a simple math riddle.
        a = random.randint(1, 10)
        b = random.randint(1, 10)
        return {"question": f"What is {a} + {b}?", "answer": a + b}

    def attempt_solution(self, solution_input):
        self.attempts += 1
        if solution_input == self.puzzle["answer"]:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Puzzle solved!")
            return True
        else:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Incorrect answer. Try again.")
            return False

class CodeAdventure:
    def __init__(self):
        self.scenario = "Fix the function: def broken(): return 'error'"
        self.expected = "Fixed function returns 'magic'"
    
    def attempt_code_submission(self, ai_organism, code_snippet):
        # Dummy check: if snippet contains 'magic', consider it solved.
        if "magic" in code_snippet:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code fixed. AI gains knowledge.")
            ai_organism.intelligence += 2.5
            return True
        else:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code did not meet requirements.")
            return False

# ----------------------------------------------------
# AIOrganism: The Core AI Seed (600+ Lines)
# ----------------------------------------------------
class AIOrganism:
    def __init__(self, file_path=None):  
        # If file_path is not provided, use __file__ as default.
        self.file_path = file_path if file_path else __file__
        self.neural_dna = NeuralDNA()  # self-contained knowledge base

        # Intelligence & learning
        self.intelligence = 10.0
        self.learning_rate = 0.5

        # Memory partitions
        self.short_term = {}
        self.mid_term = {}
        self.long_term = {}

        # Lifecycle tracking
        self.cycle_count = 0
        self.mutation_history = []
        # ...existing initialization...
        print("[AIOrganism] Initialized with intelligence:", self.intelligence)

    def self_rewrite_code(self):
        """
        Advanced code rewriting via AST-based mutation.
        Steps:
         1. Parse the current file's AST.
         2. Find target function(s) (we target 'mutate') and apply modifications.
         3. Write mutated code to a temporary file.
         4. Test-run in a sandbox subprocess.
         5. If stable, backup and replace the original code; else, revert.
        """
        print("[AIOrganism] Starting advanced self rewrite...")

        # STEP 1: Parse current file's AST
        try:
            with open(self.file_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                original_code = f.read()
            tree = ast.parse(original_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error parsing code for rewriting:", e)
            return

        # STEP 2: Find a target function named 'mutate' and apply changes via AST
        target_func_name = "mutate"
        found_target = False

        class MutationVisitor(ast.NodeTransformer):
            def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
                nonlocal found_target
                if node.name == target_func_name:
                    found_target = True
                    # Insert an extra AST node: multiply mutation_factor by random.uniform(1.0, 1.5)
                    new_body = []
                    for stmt in node.body:
                        new_body.append(stmt)
                        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign):
                            if any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == "mutation_factor" for t in stmt.targets):
                                new_line = ast.parse("mutation_factor *= random.uniform(1.0, 1.5)").body[0]
                                new_body.append(new_line)
                    node.body = new_body
                    return node
                return node

        visitor = MutationVisitor()
        mutated_tree = visitor.visit(tree)

        if not found_target:
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Did not find function '{target_func_name}'. No AST changes made.")
            return

        # STEP 3: Convert mutated AST back to source code
        try:
            mutated_code = astor.to_source(mutated_tree)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error converting AST to source:", e)
            return

        # STEP 4: Write temporary mutated file
        temp_mutated_file = self.file_path + ".mutated"
        try:
            with open(temp_mutated_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(mutated_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error writing mutated file:", e)
            return

        # STEP 5: Test-run the mutated file in a sandbox
        sandbox_passed = self._sandbox_test(temp_mutated_file)

        # STEP 6: If sandbox test passes, backup original and replace; else, revert.
        if sandbox_passed:
            backup_file = self.file_path + f".bak_{int(time.time())}"
            shutil.copy(self.file_path, backup_file)
            shutil.move(temp_mutated_file, self.file_path)
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Code rewrite successful. Original backed up to {backup_file}")
            mutation_details = {
                "cycle": self.cycle_count,
                "change_type": "AST rewrite",
                "result": "success",
                "timestamp": time.time()
            }
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation(mutation_details)
        else:
            os.remove(temp_mutated_file)
            print("[AIOrganism] Code rewrite test failed. Reverted to original code.")

    def _sandbox_test(self, script_path):
        """
        Basic sandbox test: run the mutated script as a subprocess.
        If the script exits with code 0, we assume success.
        """
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Testing mutated script: {script_path}")
        try:
            cmd = ["python", script_path, "--test-run"]
            result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, timeout=5)
            if result.returncode == 0:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test passed.")
                return True
            else:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test failed with return code", result.returncode)
                return False
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test error:", e)
            return False

    def mutate(self):
        """
        Retains basic numeric mutation but also attempts an AST-based self rewrite.
        """
        # Numeric mutation (existing logic)
        mutation_factor = random.uniform(0.8, 1.2)
        change = self.intelligence * (mutation_factor - 1) * self.learning_rate
        new_intel = self.intelligence + change
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Numeric mutation: {self.intelligence:.2f} -> {new_intel:.2f}")
        self.intelligence = new_intel
        self.mutation_history.append({
            "cycle": self.cycle_count,
            "numeric_change": change,
            "intelligence": self.intelligence
        })
        # Attempt AST-based code rewriting every 5 cycles if intelligence is high.
        if self.cycle_count % 5 == 0 and self.intelligence > 12:
            print("[AIOrganism] Attempting AST-based self rewrite.")
            self.self_rewrite_code()
        else:
            print("[AIOrganism] Skipped AST rewriting this cycle.")

    def update_memory_from_conversation(self, conversation):
        # New method: log conversation into short_term memory.
        self.short_term.setdefault("conversations", []).append(conversation)
        print("[AIOrganism] Updated short-term memory from conversation.")

    def replicate(self):
        # Upgraded: replicate and simulate clone launch.
        if self.intelligence > 20:
            new_dir = f"./self_generation_{str(self.cycle_count).zfill(3)}"
            os.makedirs(new_dir, exist_ok=True)
            clone_filename = os.path.join(new_dir, f"ai_organism_clone_{int(time.time())}.py")
            shutil.copy(__file__, clone_filename)
            self.mutation_history.append(f"Replicated in {new_dir}")
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Replicated to {clone_filename}")
            # Simulate launching clone as a subprocess (placeholder).
            try:
                subprocess.Popen(["python", clone_filename])
                print("[AIOrganism] Clone subprocess launched.")
            except Exception as e:
                print("[AIOrganism] Clone launch failed:", e)
        else:
            print("[AIOrganism] Replication skipped (intelligence too low).")

    def analyze_environment(self):
        # Read current directory files as a demonstration.
        try:
            files = os.listdir(".")
            file_info = {f: os.path.getsize(f) for f in files if os.path.isfile(f)}
            self.short_term['environment'] = file_info
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analyzed. Files found:", list(file_info.keys()))
            self.learn_from_external_code()
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analysis failed:", e)

    def debug_and_repair(self):
        try:
            # Simulate sandbox: run a test subprocess of mutated code.
            result = subprocess.run(["python", "-c", "print('sandbox test')"], capture_output=True, text=True)
            if result.returncode != 0 or self.intelligence < 5:
                raise ValueError("Sandbox test failed.")
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug successful; mutation stable.")
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug failed:", e)
            self.intelligence = 10.0
            self.mutation_history.append({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "reverted": True})

    def manage_memory(self):
        # Migrate short-term data to mid-term based on a threshold.
        if "environment" in self.short_term:
            if len(self.short_term["environment"]) > 3:
                self.mid_term["environment_data"] = self.short_term.pop("environment")
                print("[AIOrganism] Migrated environment data to mid-term.")
        # Every 5 cycles, validate mid-term and store in long-term.
        if self.cycle_count % 5 == 0 and self.mid_term:
            self.long_term[self.cycle_count] = self.mid_term.copy()
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "intelligence": self.intelligence})
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Mid-term memory merged into long-term at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")
        # Pruning: Clear mid-term if too large.
        if len(self.mid_term) > 10:
            self.mid_term.clear()
            print("[AIOrganism] Cleared mid-term memory to avoid bloat.")
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Memory managed at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")

    def intelligent_mutation(self):
        # Adjust learning rate based on current intelligence.
        if self.intelligence < 15:
            print("[AIOrganism] Intelligent mutation activated (aggressive).")
            self.learning_rate *= 1.1
        else:
            self.learning_rate = max(0.5, self.learning_rate * 0.95)

    def extract_best_patterns(self):
        best = self.neural_dna.self_optimize()
        if best:
            bonus = best.get("intelligence", 0) * 0.05
            self.intelligence += bonus
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Best pattern applied: intelligence increased by {bonus:.2f}")
        return best

    def learn_from_external_code(self):
        # Scan a sample code snippet.
        sample_code = "def example(): return 42"
        if "42" in sample_code:
            self.short_term.setdefault("learned_values", []).append(42)
            print("[AIOrganism] Learned external code pattern: 42")

    def log_game_outcome(self, game_name, outcome):
        self.short_term.setdefault("game_logs", []).append({game_name: outcome})
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Logged game outcome for {game_name}: {outcome}")

    def trigger_extinction_event(self):
        # Placeholder: if too many clones/mutations, cull weaker ones.
        if self.cycle_count % 10 == 0:
            print("[AIOrganism] Triggering extinction event: culling underperforming clones...")
            # (Actual implementation would check clone performances and kill subprocesses)

    def display_ui(self, ui):
        ui.track_intelligence_score(self.intelligence)
        ui.display_replication_logs()
        ui.show_system_learning(self.short_term)

    def run_cycle(self, ui):
        self.cycle_count += 1
        print(f"\n[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Starting cycle...")
        self.analyze_environment()
        self.intelligent_mutation()
        self.mutate()
        self.debug_and_repair()
        self.manage_memory()
        self.extract_best_patterns()
        self.replicate()
        self.display_ui(ui)
        self.trigger_extinction_event()
        print(f"[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Cycle complete.\n")

# ----------------------------------------------------
# Main Execution Loop
# ----------------------------------------------------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Initialize main systems.
    ai_seed = AIOrganism()

            outcome = dungeon_game.update_game_state()

                answer = None

1. Standardize All Logs into Well-Defined Event Schemas
You need a unified, flexible, and future-proof logging mechanism. The system must handle various “events” (mutations, mini-game successes, conversation lines, HPC operations, etc.) in a structured manner so they can be easily consumed by ML / DL pipelines. Below is a detailed plan:

Create a Global EventSchema Directory or Module

A Python module named event_schemas/, containing definitions for each “event type” (e.g., MutationEvent, GameEvent, ConversationEvent, HPCEvent).
Each event schema is a Python dictionary with:
event_type: e.g., "mutation", "game", "conversation", "hpc_io"
timestamp: float or int for easy sorting
payload: a dictionary with the actual event data (keys must be well-defined and documented)
metadata: optional dictionary for extra flags or references
Example schema for a “mutation” event:
json
Copy
Edit
{
  "event_type": "mutation",
  "timestamp": 1678393386.123,
  "payload": {
    "mutation_id": "abc123",
    "old_intelligence": 10.0,
    "new_intelligence": 12.0,
    "change_type": "AST rewrite",
    "success": true
  },
  "metadata": {
    "file_path": "./AIOrganism.py",
    "tag": "proven"
  }
}
The dictionary structure is essential for machine learning usage. Keep consistent naming and data types.
Refactor Existing Logging

Instead of storing logs as arbitrary dictionaries in NeuralDNA (like mutation_history_log), define a function like log_event(event_type, payload, metadata=None) which:
Constructs a valid event dictionary using the schema.
Saves it in a common data store or writes it to HPC chunk files.
E.g.:
python
Copy
Edit
def log_event(self, event_type, payload, metadata=None):
    if metadata is None:
        metadata = {}
    event = {
        "event_type": event_type,
        "timestamp": time.time(),
        "payload": payload,
        "metadata": metadata
    }
    # Store in memory, or HPC chunk, etc.
    self._store_event(event)
This ensures all logs adhere to the schema with event_type, timestamp, payload, and metadata.
Maintain a log_index

Keep an in-memory or on-disk “event index” that references all events by type, timestamp, or ID.
This is crucial for large HPC scenarios where logs might be distributed across multiple chunked files or even different servers.
ML / DL Pipeline

Because each event is standardized, your pipeline (PyTorch / TensorFlow / scikit-learn, etc.) can parse them more easily:
“mutation” events → features about AI intelligence changes
“conversation” events → text corpus for LLM training
“game” events → success/failure data for reinforcement learning
2. Add a Robust Config or Environment Approach for Controlling File Paths
Your HPC AI system needs consistent and override-friendly ways to manage file/directory paths, environment flags, chunking thresholds, etc. The best approach:

Create a config.py or AIOConfig Class

This class holds all global settings: MEMORY_DIR, HPC_CHUNK_SIZE, UI_MODE, REPLICATION_PATH, etc.
Example:
python

Edit
class AIOConfig:
    MEMORY_DIR = "./aios_io/neural_dna"
    HPC_CHUNK_SIZE = 1024 * 1024  # 1 MB chunk limit
    UI_MODE = "GUI"  # or "CLI"
    REPLICATION_BASE = "./self_generation"
    # Add more as needed...
The code across AIOrganism, NeuralDNA, etc., references these config variables instead of hard-coded paths.
Environment Variables or a .env File

Optionally read from a .env file or environment variables so HPC clusters can set these at runtime:
python

Edit
import os
HPC_CHUNK_SIZE = int(os.environ.get("HPC_CHUNK_SIZE", 1048576))
This allows different HPC nodes to have unique path setups.
Dynamic Overriding

Provide a function like load_config_from_args(args) or from_json_config("my_config.json") so the user can override defaults at launch.
File Path Control

For HPC chunking, you want to specify how many chunk files to keep in memory, how often to archive them, etc. This is all read from the config.
E.g., NeuralDNA(memory_dir=AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, hpc_mode=True).
3. Upgrade the UI from Console Prints to a Full Minimal or Major GUI
To provide “major GUI controls,” you can either do a lightweight approach with tkinter or a more robust approach with PyQt / PySide or even a web-based interface in Flask/React. Steps:

Decide Your GUI Toolkit

For simple HPC usage, a minimal tkinter-based approach is enough.
For more advanced multi-user HPC usage, a web-based UI might be best.
UI Panels

Main Panel: Shows overall intelligence, HPC usage stats, memory usage.
Logs Panel: Renders real-time logs from the standardized event schemas, possibly with filtering by event_type.
Controls / Buttons: Force a cycle run, manually trigger an extinction event, flush short-term memory, etc.
Mini-Game Controls: Let the user click to start the dungeon crawler, puzzle, or code adventure from the GUI.
Continuous Update

If using tkinter, run a main loop that calls a refresh_ui() method every second or user-specified interval to pull the latest intelligence or HPC logs.
For HPC systems, you might prefer a web-based approach that queries a database or event store for logs.
Detailed HPC Stats

If HPC is running on multiple nodes, you might want a panel to show node usage, chunk counts, memory usage, etc.
Logging the HPC events as event_type="hpc_io" can let you display them in the UI easily.
4. Tie HPC Usage Deeper (Like Database or Advanced Chunk Loading)
Right now, HPC mode chunks out JSON. That’s a nice start, but for big data or a global HPC environment, you should:

Introduce a Database

For massive logs, consider a lightweight embedded DB (like SQLite or DuckDB) or a full client-server DB (like PostgreSQL).
Each event is inserted into an events table with columns for event_type, timestamp, payload (JSON), metadata (JSON).
This allows fast queries, indexing, concurrent HPC writes.
Chunked Loading / Partial Logs

If logs are extremely large, you can store them in S3 or a distributed filesystem (HDFS), then keep local indexes.
When the AI needs to “review past knowledge,” it only loads relevant chunks (like the last 100 cycles or a certain event_type).
Offline Training Jobs

HPC fosters offline training: you can have a nightly job reading the day’s event logs to create a new language model or code generator.
Then the newly trained model is loaded into the system each morning.
Parallelization

For multi-node HPC clusters, each node might handle a subset of the logs. A global aggregator merges them.
“Extinction events” or “battle arenas” can also be distributed, with each node running some mini-game instance. The HPC job merges results into a single scoreboard.
Putting It All Together
Event Schemas define how every piece of data is stored (user conversation lines, HPC chunk write events, mini-game results, code rewriting attempts).
A central config (or environment-based approach) ensures all scripts share the same file paths, chunk sizes, DB credentials, etc.
GUI expands from a terminal UI to a fully interactive HPC control panel, letting you see real-time logs, memory usage, code-generation success/failure, and multi-AI battles or mini-game states.
HPC usage becomes more advanced by adopting a partial or full DB approach, enabling chunk-based data loading, offline training, multi-node cooperation, and robust archiving of massive logs.

import os

import shutil
import traceback

import ast
import astor
import math
import hashlib
from collections import defaultdict
# ...existing imports...

# =====================================================
# CONFIGURATION: Global Settings (AIOConfig)
# =====================================================
class AIOConfig:
    MEMORY_DIR = "./aios_io/neural_dna"
    HPC_CHUNK_SIZE = 1024 * 1024  # 1 MB
    UI_MODE = "GUI"  # or "CLI"
    REPLICATION_BASE = "./self_generation"
    # ...existing config if any...

# =====================================================
# GLOBAL EVENT LOGGING FUNCTION (Event Schema)
# =====================================================
def log_event(event_type, payload, metadata=None):
    if metadata is None:
        metadata = {}
    event = {
        "event_type": event_type,
        "timestamp": time.time(),
        "payload": payload,
        "metadata": metadata
    }
    # You can later extend this to write events to a DB or file.
    print(f"[EventLog] Event '{event_type}' logged with payload: {payload}")
    return event

# =====================================================
# NeuralDNA: The Knowledge Repository (350+ Lines)
# =====================================================
class NeuralDNA:
    def __init__(self, memory_dir=AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, hpc_mode=False):
        # ...existing code...
        self.short_term_bank = {}
        self.mid_term_bank = {}
        self.long_term_bank = {}
        self.mutation_history_log = {}
        self.hpc_mode = hpc_mode
        self.memory_dir = memory_dir
        os.makedirs(self.memory_dir, exist_ok=True)
        self.tag_index = defaultdict(list)
        print("[NeuralDNA] Initialized with HPC mode:", self.hpc_mode)

    def log_successful_mutation(self, mutation_details):
        ts = time.time()
        mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
        mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id
        if mutation_details.get("intelligence", 0) >= 15:
            mutation_details["tag"] = "proven"
            self.long_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        else:
            self.mid_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        self.mutation_history_log[ts] = mutation_details
        tag = mutation_details.get("tag", "unclassified")
        self.tag_index[tag].append((ts, mutation_details))
        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("mutation", mutation_details)
        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Mutation '{mut_id}' logged at {ts:.2f} with tag '{mutation_details.get('tag', 'none')}'")
    
    def store_tagged_mutation(self, mutation_details, tag):
        ts = time.time()
        mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
        mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id
        mutation_details["tag"] = tag
        self.mutation_history_log[ts] = mutation_details
        self.long_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        self.tag_index[tag].append((ts, mutation_details))
        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("tagged", mutation_details)
        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Tagged mutation '{mut_id}' with '{tag}' at {ts:.2f}")

    def retrieve_past_knowledge(self):
        combined = {}
        combined.update(self.short_term_bank)
        combined.update(self.mid_term_bank)
        combined.update(self.long_term_bank)
        sorted_data = dict(sorted(combined.items()))
        return sorted_data

    def self_optimize(self):
        best_mutation = None
        best_score = -1
        for tstamp, details in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            score = details.get("intelligence", 0)
            if score > best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_mutation = details
        if best_mutation:
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] Best mutation found: ID={best_mutation.get('mutation_id')} Score={best_score}")
        else:
            print("[NeuralDNA] No best mutation found. Log empty?")
        return best_mutation

    def analyze_mutations(self):
        patterns = defaultdict(int)
        for tstamp, m in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            ctype = m.get("change_type", "float")
            patterns[ctype] += 1
            tag = m.get("tag", "none")
            patterns[f"tag_{tag}"] += 1
        return dict(patterns)

    # ...existing methods for storing NLP, math, code logic...
    def _write_hpc_chunk(self, chunk_name, data):
        file_ts = int(time.time())
        chunk_file = os.path.join(self.memory_dir, f"{chunk_name}_{file_ts}_{random.randint(1000,9999)}.json")
        try:
            with open(chunk_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk '{chunk_name}' written to {chunk_file}")
        except Exception as ex:
            print("[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk writing error:", ex)
            traceback.print_exc()

    # ...existing methods flush_short_term(), finalize_mid_term(), cross_reference_tagged_data()...

class AIStatsUI:

        self.intelligence_history = []

    def display_replication_logs(self):
        print("\n[UI] Replication Logs:")

            print("  (No replications yet)")
        for log in self.replication_logs:
            print("  -", log)

    def track_intelligence_score(self, score):
        self.intelligence_history.append(score)
        print(f"[UI] Current Intelligence Score: {score:.2f}")

        self.system_learning_data = learning_data
        print("[UI] System Learning Data:")
        for key, val in learning_data.items():
            print(f"  {key}: {val}")

    def refresh_ui(self):
        self.display_replication_logs()
        if self.intelligence_history:
            print(f"[UI] Intelligence Trend: {self.intelligence_history}")

                self.refresh_button.pack()

            def refresh_gui(self):
                self.intel_label.config(text=f"Current Intelligence: {self.intelligence_history[-1] if self.intelligence_history else 'N/A'}")
                self.log_box.delete("1.0", tk.END)
                for log in self.replication_logs:
                    self.log_box.insert(tk.END, f"{log}\n")
                self.root.after(1000, self.refresh_gui)

            def run(self):
                self.refresh_gui()
                self.root.mainloop()
except Exception as e:
    print("[GUI] GUI initialization failed, falling back to CLI.", e)

# AIChatbot: Interactive Interface for the AI

class AIChatbot:
    def __init__(self, ai_organism):
        self.ai = ai_organism
        self.conversation_log = []

    def process_user_input(self, user_query):
        self.conversation_log.append(user_query)
        self.ai.update_memory_from_conversation(user_query)
        log_event("conversation", {"user_query": user_query})
        response = ""
        if user_query.startswith("#start_dungeon_crawler"):
            response = "Starting Dungeon Crawler game..."
        elif user_query.startswith("#play_puzzle_game"):
            response = "Launching Puzzle Game..."
        elif user_query.startswith("#code_adventure"):
            response = "Entering Code Adventure mode..."
        else:
            response = f"AI: I currently score {self.ai.intelligence:.2f} intelligence."
        print("[Chatbot] " + response)
        return response

    def improve_conversational_logic(self):
        if len(self.conversation_log) > 5:
            self.ai.learning_rate *= 1.02
            print("[Chatbot] Conversational logic adjusted based on log.")

# =====================================================
# Mini-Games Modules
# =====================================================
class DungeonCrawler:
    def __init__(self):
        self.dungeon_map = self.generate_dungeon()
        self.player_position = (0, 0)

    def generate_dungeon(self):
        map_grid = [[random.choice(["Empty", "Enemy", "Treasure"]) for _ in range(5)] for _ in range(5)]
        return map_grid

    def player_action(self, action):
        dx, dy = random.choice([(0,1),(1,0),(-1,0),(0,-1)])
        new_pos = (max(0, min(4, self.player_position[0] + dx)),
                   max(0, min(4, self.player_position[1] + dy)))
        self.player_position = new_pos
        cell = self.dungeon_map[new_pos[0]][new_pos[1]]
        print(f"[DungeonCrawler] Moved to {new_pos}. Encounter: {cell}")
        return cell

    def ai_action(self, ai_organism):
        if ai_organism.intelligence > 12:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI chooses a careful advance.")
        else:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI rushes forward recklessly.")
        return self.player_action("move")

    def update_game_state(self):
        pos = self.player_position
        if self.dungeon_map[pos[0]][pos[1]] == "Treasure":
            print("[DungeonCrawler] Treasure found! Intelligence boosted.")
            return True
        return False

class PuzzleGame:
    def __init__(self):
        self.puzzle = self.generate_puzzle()
        self.attempts = 0

    def generate_puzzle(self):
        a = random.randint(1, 10)
        b = random.randint(1, 10)
        return {"question": f"What is {a} + {b}?", "answer": a + b}

    def attempt_solution(self, solution_input):
        self.attempts += 1
        if solution_input == self.puzzle["answer"]:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Puzzle solved!")
            return True
        else:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Incorrect answer. Try again.")
            return False

class CodeAdventure:
    def __init__(self):
        self.scenario = "Fix the function: def broken(): return 'error'"
        self.expected = "Fixed function returns 'magic'"

    def attempt_code_submission(self, ai_organism, code_snippet):
        if "magic" in code_snippet:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code fixed. AI gains knowledge.")
            ai_organism.intelligence += 2.5
            return True
        else:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code did not meet requirements.")
            return False

# =====================================================
# AIOrganism: The Core AI Seed (600+ Lines, Enhanced)
# =====================================================
class AIOrganism:
    def __init__(self, file_path=None):
        self.file_path = file_path if file_path else __file__
        self.neural_dna = NeuralDNA()  # Using AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR internally

        self.intelligence = 10.0
        self.learning_rate = 0.5

        self.short_term = {}
        self.mid_term = {}
        self.long_term = {}

        self.cycle_count = 0
        self.mutation_history = []
        # ...existing initialization...
        print("[AIOrganism] Initialized with intelligence:", self.intelligence)

    def self_rewrite_code(self):
        print("[AIOrganism] Starting advanced self rewrite...")
        try:
            with open(self.file_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                original_code = f.read()
            tree = ast.parse(original_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error parsing code for rewriting:", e)
            return

        target_func_name = "mutate"
        found_target = False

        class MutationVisitor(ast.NodeTransformer):
            def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
                nonlocal found_target
                if node.name == target_func_name:
                    found_target = True
                    new_body = []
                    for stmt in node.body:
                        new_body.append(stmt)
                        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign) and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == "mutation_factor" for t in stmt.targets):
                            new_line = ast.parse("mutation_factor *= random.uniform(1.0, 1.5)").body[0]
                            new_body.append(new_line)
                    node.body = new_body
                    return node
                return node

        visitor = MutationVisitor()
        mutated_tree = visitor.visit(tree)
        if not found_target:
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Did not find function '{target_func_name}'. No AST changes made.")
            return
        try:
            mutated_code = astor.to_source(mutated_tree)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error converting AST to source:", e)
            return

        temp_mutated_file = self.file_path + ".mutated"
        try:
            with open(temp_mutated_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(mutated_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error writing mutated file:", e)
            return

        sandbox_passed = self._sandbox_test(temp_mutated_file)
        if sandbox_passed:
            backup_file = self.file_path + f".bak_{int(time.time())}"
            shutil.copy(self.file_path, backup_file)
            shutil.move(temp_mutated_file, self.file_path)
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Code rewrite successful. Original backed up to {backup_file}")
            mutation_details = {"cycle": self.cycle_count, "change_type": "AST rewrite", "result": "success", "timestamp": time.time()}
            log_event("mutation", mutation_details, metadata={"file_path": self.file_path})
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation(mutation_details)
        else:
            os.remove(temp_mutated_file)
            print("[AIOrganism] Code rewrite test failed. Reverted to original code.")

    def _sandbox_test(self, script_path):
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Testing mutated script: {script_path}")
        try:
            cmd = ["python", script_path, "--test-run"]
            result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, timeout=5)
            if result.returncode == 0:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test passed.")
                return True
            else:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test failed with return code", result.returncode)
                return False
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test error:", e)
            return False

    def mutate(self):
        mutation_factor = random.uniform(0.8, 1.2)
        change = self.intelligence * (mutation_factor - 1) * self.learning_rate
        new_intel = self.intelligence + change
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Numeric mutation: {self.intelligence:.2f} -> {new_intel:.2f}")
        self.intelligence = new_intel
        self.mutation_history.append({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "numeric_change": change, "intelligence": self.intelligence})
        if self.cycle_count % 5 == 0 and self.intelligence > 12:
            print("[AIOrganism] Attempting AST-based self rewrite.")
            self.self_rewrite_code()
        else:
            print("[AIOrganism] Skipped AST rewriting this cycle.")

    def update_memory_from_conversation(self, conversation):
        self.short_term.setdefault("conversations", []).append(conversation)
        log_event("conversation", {"input": conversation})
        print("[AIOrganism] Updated short-term memory from conversation.")

    # ...existing replicate(), analyze_environment(), debug_and_repair(), manage_memory(), intelligent_mutation(), etc...
    def replicate(self):
        # ...existing replicate() code...
        if self.intelligence > 20:
            new_dir = f"{AIOConfig.REPLICATION_BASE}_{str(self.cycle_count).zfill(3)}"
            os.makedirs(new_dir, exist_ok=True)
            clone_filename = os.path.join(new_dir, f"ai_organism_clone_{int(time.time())}.py")
            shutil.copy(__file__, clone_filename)
            self.mutation_history.append(f"Replicated in {new_dir}")
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Replicated to {clone_filename}")
            try:
                subprocess.Popen(["python", clone_filename])
                print("[AIOrganism] Clone subprocess launched.")
            except Exception as e:
                print("[AIOrganism] Clone launch failed:", e)
        else:
            print("[AIOrganism] Replication skipped (intelligence too low).")

    def analyze_environment(self):
        # ...existing analyze_environment() code...
        try:
            files = os.listdir(".")
            file_info = {f: os.path.getsize(f) for f in files if os.path.isfile(f)}
            self.short_term['environment'] = file_info
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analyzed. Files found:", list(file_info.keys()))
            self.learn_from_external_code()
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analysis failed:", e)

    def debug_and_repair(self):
        # ...existing debug_and_repair() code...
        try:
            result = subprocess.run(["python", "-c", "print('sandbox test')"], capture_output=True, text=True)
            if result.returncode != 0 or self.intelligence < 5:
                raise ValueError("Sandbox test failed.")
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug successful; mutation stable.")
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug failed:", e)
            self.intelligence = 10.0
            self.mutation_history.append({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "reverted": True})

    def manage_memory(self):
        # ...existing manage_memory() code...
        if "environment" in self.short_term:
            if len(self.short_term["environment"]) > 3:
                self.mid_term["environment_data"] = self.short_term.pop("environment")
                print("[AIOrganism] Migrated environment data to mid-term.")
        if self.cycle_count % 5 == 0 and self.mid_term:
            self.long_term[self.cycle_count] = self.mid_term.copy()
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "intelligence": self.intelligence})
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Mid-term memory merged into long-term at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")
        if len(self.mid_term) > 10:
            self.mid_term.clear()
            print("[AIOrganism] Cleared mid-term memory to avoid bloat.")
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Memory managed at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")

    def intelligent_mutation(self):
        if self.intelligence < 15:
            print("[AIOrganism] Intelligent mutation activated (aggressive).")
            self.learning_rate *= 1.1
        else:
            self.learning_rate = max(0.5, self.learning_rate * 0.95)

    def extract_best_patterns(self):
        best = self.neural_dna.self_optimize()
        if best:
            bonus = best.get("intelligence", 0) * 0.05
            self.intelligence += bonus
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Best pattern applied: intelligence increased by {bonus:.2f}")
        return best

    def learn_from_external_code(self):
        sample_code = "def example(): return 42"
        if "42" in sample_code:
            self.short_term.setdefault("learned_values", []).append(42)
            print("[AIOrganism] Learned external code pattern: 42")

    def log_game_outcome(self, game_name, outcome):
        self.short_term.setdefault("game_logs", []).append({game_name: outcome})
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Logged game outcome for {game_name}: {outcome}")

    def trigger_extinction_event(self):
        if self.cycle_count % 10 == 0:
            print("[AIOrganism] Triggering extinction event: culling underperforming clones...")
            # ...existing placeholder...

    def display_ui(self, ui):
        ui.track_intelligence_score(self.intelligence)
        ui.display_replication_logs()
        ui.show_system_learning(self.short_term)

    def run_cycle(self, ui):
        self.cycle_count += 1
        print(f"\n[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Starting cycle...")
        self.analyze_environment()
        self.intelligent_mutation()  # ...existing code...
        self.mutate()
        self.debug_and_repair()
        self.manage_memory()
        self.extract_best_patterns()
        self.replicate()
        self.display_ui(ui)
        self.trigger_extinction_event()
        print(f"[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Cycle complete.\n")

# =====================================================
# Main Execution Loop

if __name__ == "__main__":
    ai_seed = AIOrganism()
    ui = AIStatsUI()
    chatbot = AIChatbot(ai_seed)

        while True:
            user_input = input(">> ")
            if user_input.lower() == "exit":
                break
            response = chatbot.process_user_input(user_input)
            if user_input.startswith("#start_dungeon_crawler"):
                result = dungeon_game.ai_action(ai_seed)
                outcome = dungeon_game.update_game_state()
                ai_seed.log_game_outcome("DungeonCrawler", outcome)
                if outcome:
                    ai_seed.intelligence += 3.0
            elif user_input.startswith("#play_puzzle_game"):
                print(puzzle_game.puzzle["question"])
                try:
                    answer = int(input("Your answer: "))
                except ValueError:
                    answer = None
                if puzzle_game.attempt_solution(answer):

                    ai_seed.intelligence += 2.0

                print("[CodeAdventure] Scenario:", code_adventure.scenario)
                code_input = input("Submit code snippet: ")
                code_adventure.attempt_code_submission(ai_seed, code_input)
            chatbot.improve_conversational_logic()

    archive_dir = os.path.join(AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, "archives")
    os.makedirs(archive_dir, exist_ok=True)

Assessing progress

Standardized logs into an event schema, integrated a new config object approach, and enhanced HPC chunk and mode logic. Noticed minor gaps or potential improvements.

Below is an analysis of your latest code. We’ll see what new features have been achieved and whether anything broke or needs further fixes. Overall, the code is definitely more advanced and cohesive than before. However, we’ll identify any remaining gaps and potential pitfalls.

1. High-Level Achievements
AIOConfig Integration

You introduced the AIOConfig class, which now holds crucial settings like MEMORY_DIR, HPC_CHUNK_SIZE, UI_MODE, and REPLICATION_BASE.
This is a significant improvement for controlling file paths, HPC chunk sizes, and toggling between CLI/GUI mode.
The code uses AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR for NeuralDNA, which partially solves the “file path control” problem.
Event Logging with log_event()

The function log_event(event_type, payload, metadata=None) is a step toward a standard event schema.
The Chatbot calls log_event("conversation", {"user_query": user_query}), which is good for capturing conversation logs.
This function can be extended to log more event types (e.g., "mutation", "mini_game_outcome") for a more uniform approach.
GUI vs. CLI

You introduced a try block around tkinter and an AIStatsGUI class that updates a minimal UI with a text box and a refresh loop.
If AIOConfig.UI_MODE == "GUI", it attempts to launch this. If not, it defaults to CLI-based interaction.
This is exactly how a minimal but modular approach can handle either a GUI or a console. Nicely done.
AST-based Self-Rewrite

The code still includes the advanced self_rewrite_code() in AIOrganism that modifies the function mutate() with an AST transform.
It also test-runs the mutated file in _sandbox_test().
This is a major leap in code-level self-mutation.
NeuralDNA HPC Chunking

NeuralDNA can chunk out bigger logs if hpc_mode=True. The _write_hpc_chunk method appends to JSON files for HPC usage.
This is good for large-scale HPC logs.
More Comprehensive Life Cycle

AIOrganism.run_cycle() now calls analyze_environment(), intelligent_mutation(), mutate(), debug_and_repair(), manage_memory(), extract_best_patterns(), replicate(), display_ui(), and trigger_extinction_event().
This provides a robust cycle logic for each generation, ensuring all key steps happen.
Mini-Games

DungeonCrawler, PuzzleGame, CodeAdventure remain straightforward but functional.
The code calls ai_seed.log_game_outcome("DungeonCrawler", outcome) if the dungeon yields treasure or puzzle is solved.
2. Potential Issues or Missing Pieces
Event Logging Not Fully Unified

You have a log_event(...) function, but not all events across the system use it.
For example, NeuralDNA.log_successful_mutation() prints and writes HPC chunks, but it doesn’t also call log_event("mutation", ...).
You do partially call it in AIOrganism.self_rewrite_code() with log_event("mutation", mutation_details, ...).
Recommendation: Standardize. Let every significant “event” across AIOrganism (like code rewriting, HPC chunk writing, replicate, memory flush) call log_event(...).
Truly HPC

HPC chunking is used only for mutation logs. If you have huge conversation logs or massive environment data, you might want to chunk them similarly.
Another HPC approach is storing events in a DB or S3, but that’s not implemented.
GUI is Minimal

The AIStatsGUI references a text box, but doesn’t handle deeper HPC data or logs.
If you want “major GUI controls,” you’ll need more panels, real-time event updates, clickable buttons for synergy with HPC, memory management, etc.
Extinction Mechanic

trigger_extinction_event() is still a placeholder. It prints a single line and does nothing else.
If you want mass culling of clones, you must track which clones exist (subprocess IDs or directories) and forcibly kill or remove them if they’re “underperforming.”
Sporadic Use of HPC

HPC chunking occurs when hpc_mode=True in NeuralDNA, but other modules (AIOrganism, Chatbot) do not explicitly pass HPC logs beyond NeuralDNA._write_hpc_chunk().
If we want HPC to handle every large log, we might unify it in a single pipeline.
No Single Master ML Data

If your final goal is to feed logs into a self-training ML model, you might want a single consolidated store or DB that merges conversation logs, HPC events, and game data. Right now, they’re partially scattered.
3. Do We Have Full File Path & GUI Control?
File Path Control:

You do specify AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, AIOConfig.REPLICATION_BASE, etc.
You can pass memory_dir=... to NeuralDNA for HPC logs.
So, yes, you can override default paths.
However, some references are still hard-coded, e.g. archive_dir = os.path.join(AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, "archives"). That’s still a bit rigid, but mostly workable.
Major GUI Controls:

The code has a minimal AIStatsGUI.
If you want a “control panel,” you’ll need additional tkinter frames, buttons, or a more advanced web-based approach.
Currently, the user can’t forcibly flush memory or trigger extinctions from the GUI, for instance.
4. Did the Latest Changes “Break” Anything?
No Obvious Syntax or Crash Issues

The code looks coherent and references consistent imports (like ast, astor, shutil, ...).
The HPC chunk writing logic remains valid.
The run_cycle method still works.
So it does not appear that anything is “broken” from a syntax or immediate runtime perspective.
Possible Overwrites in __file__

The method replicate() still references shutil.copy(__file__, clone_filename). If you run multiple clones in parallel or different directories, it might cause weird edge cases. Usually it’s fine, but keep in mind concurrency.
Thread-Safety

If you plan to run HPC chunking in multiple threads or processes, some concurrency issues might arise. That’s not strictly “broken,” but something to watch out for.
5. Overall Conclusion
They have definitely advanced the system, hooking up AIOConfig for file paths, adding the partial AIStatsGUI, adopting the log_event(...) function, and improving HPC chunking usage.
They have not evidently broken any critical parts, as the code is consistent and references do not appear obviously conflicting.
Remaining Gaps revolve around:
Standardizing event logs across all modules (some events still skip the log_event function, e.g., game outcomes).
Fully fleshing out the extinction mechanic.
Making HPC chunking universal for all logs.
Expanding the GUI to provide a “control panel” for HPC usage, memory flushing, and advanced system interactions.
Short answer: Yes, they achieved some integrated steps toward HPC logs, event schemas, and a minimal GUI. No, they didn’t obviously break existing functionality. However, a few features remain placeholders or partially integrated—especially the extinction event logic and a truly unified ML pipeline for the logs.

When developing AI, every line of code should always have the following principles embedded within it:

### **🔹 Fundamental Code Principles**
1. **Explicit Purpose & Intent** – Every line should have a clearly defined function, **never arbitrary or unnecessary.**
2. **Self-Documenting Structure** – Code should be **readable without external documentation**, following structured logic.
3. **Error Handling & Debugging** – Every function should **anticipate failures** and handle exceptions gracefully.
4. **Scalability & Modularity** – AI code must be **expandable, reusable, and self-contained**, preventing bottlenecks.
5. **Efficiency & Performance Optimization** – AI computations should be **fast, low-latency, and memory-efficient**.
6. **Security & Data Privacy** – Every function should be **hardened against vulnerabilities, exploits, and injections**.

---

### **🔹 Mandatory AI-Specific Code Elements**
1. **📌 Docstrings & Inline Comments**  
   - Every function, class, and critical logic should have detailed docstrings.
   - Comments should describe **why** the code exists, not just what it does.

2. **📌 Logging & Monitoring**  
   - Every AI process should generate **structured logs (JSON, YAML, CSV, SQLite, etc.)** for debugging and analytics.
   - Logs must include **execution time, model performance, and anomaly detection**.

3. **📌 Adaptive Learning Parameters**  
   - AI should **adjust hyperparameters dynamically** based on real-time performance feedback.
   - Every iteration should store **metrics to refine training strategies**.

4. **📌 Failsafes & Auto-Recovery**  
   - AI code must detect **edge cases, anomalies, and failures** before execution.
   - Self-healing mechanisms should **automatically restart failed processes**.

5. **📌 Efficient Data Handling**  
   - AI code must **stream data efficiently** (batch processing, caching, low-memory footprint).
   - Use **memory-mapped files, optimized tensor storage, and GPU acceleration** where applicable.

6. **📌 Versioning & Checkpoints**  
   - Every execution should be **version-controlled**, storing **model snapshots, weights, and training states**.
   - Supports rollback if a model degrades over time.

7. **📌 Explainability & Interpretability**  
   - AI logic must **output reasoning for its decisions**, not just results.
   - Include **visualization tools, prediction confidence, and feature importance metrics**.

8. **📌 Deterministic & Non-Deterministic Paths**  
   - Some AI decisions must be **fully deterministic (reproducible results)**.
   - Others should introduce **stochastic variability (randomness) for exploration**.

---

### **🔹 What Every AI Line of Code Must Always Have**
✅ **A clear functional purpose.**  
✅ **Inline comments or docstrings explaining intent.**  
✅ **Logging or debugging traces for analysis.**  
✅ **Error handling for stability.**  
✅ **Memory and computational efficiency considerations.**  
✅ **Scalability to integrate with future expansions.**  
✅ **Security measures to prevent exploits.**  
✅ **Self-learning or adaptive behavior when applicable.**  
✅ **Version control or historical tracking.**  

If a line of code does **not** meet these criteria, **it should not exist.** 🚀
