class SecurityManager:
    """
    Manages security aspects of the AI system including:
    - Input sanitization
    - Access control
    - Mutation verification
    - HPC security boundaries
    
    This class ensures system integrity while allowing controlled evolution.
    """
    def __init__(self):
        self.operation_locks = {}  # Thread-safe operation management
        self.verified_mutations = set()  # Cryptographically verified changes
        self.security_log = []
        self.thread_pool = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4)
    
    def verify_code_mutation(self, code: str, mutation_id: str) -> bool:
        """
        Verifies code mutations for security and integrity.
        Prevents malicious code injection and ensures cosmic harmony.
        """
        # Generate cryptographic hash of code
        code_hash = hashlib.sha256(code.encode()).hexdigest()
        
        # Security checks
        if self._contains_dangerous_patterns(code):
            self.log_security_event("dangerous_pattern_detected", mutation_id)
            return False
            
        # Verify mutation doesn't break system boundaries
        if self._verify_system_boundaries(code):
            self.verified_mutations.add(mutation_id)
            return True
        return False

    def _contains_dangerous_patterns(self, code: str) -> bool:
        """Checks for potentially harmful code patterns."""
        dangerous_patterns = {
            'os.system', 'subprocess.shell',
            'eval(', 'exec(',
            '__import__('
        }
        return any(pattern in code for pattern in dangerous_patterns)

    def _verify_system_boundaries(self, code: str) -> bool:
        """Ensures code mutations maintain system stability."""
        # Add your boundary verification logic here
        return True

    def log_security_event(self, event_type: str, details: Any) -> None:
        """Records security-related events for analysis."""
        self.security_log.append({
            'timestamp': time.time(),
            'type': event_type,
            'details': details
        })

class MetaLearningSystem:
    """
    Implements advanced self-learning capabilities:
    - Pattern recognition in mutation success/failure
    - Intelligence optimization strategies
    - Universal model alignment
    """
    def __init__(self, db_manager: DatabaseManager):
        self.db = db_manager
        self.learning_patterns = {}
        self.intelligence_history = []
        self.pattern_weights = np.ones(10)  # Initial equal weights

    async def analyze_mutation_patterns(self) -> Dict[str, float]:
        """
        Analyzes patterns in successful mutations to guide future evolution.
        Integrates with the universal model for optimal growth paths.
        """
        mutations = await self.db.get_events("mutation", limit=1000)
        
        # Calculate success patterns
        pattern_scores = defaultdict(list)
        for mutation in mutations:
            if mutation['success']:
                pattern = self._extract_pattern(mutation)
                score = mutation['intelligence_gain']
                pattern_scores[pattern].append(score)
        
        # Update pattern weights using entropy
        self._update_pattern_weights(pattern_scores)
        
        return {p: np.mean(scores) for p, scores in pattern_scores.items()}

    def _extract_pattern(self, mutation: Dict) -> str:
        """
        Extracts meaningful patterns from mutation data.
        Considers both logical and universal aspects of the change.
        """
        # Your pattern extraction logic here
        return "pattern"

    def _update_pattern_weights(self, pattern_scores: Dict[str, List[float]]) -> None:
        """
        Updates pattern weights based on their effectiveness.
        Uses entropy to measure pattern information content.
        """
        # Calculate entropy for each pattern
        pattern_entropy = []
        for scores in pattern_scores.values():
            hist, _ = np.histogram(scores, bins=10, density=True)
            pattern_entropy.append(entropy(hist + 1e-10))
        
        # Update weights based on entropy
        if pattern_entropy:
            self.pattern_weights = np.array(pattern_entropy) / np.sum(pattern_entropy)

class NeuralDNA:
    def __init__(self, memory_dir=AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR, hpc_mode=False):
        # ...existing code...
        self.short_term_bank = {}
        self.mid_term_bank = {}
        self.long_term_bank = {}
        self.mutation_history_log = {}
        self.hpc_mode = hpc_mode
        self.memory_dir = memory_dir
        os.makedirs(self.memory_dir, exist_ok=True)
        self.tag_index = defaultdict(list)
        self.db = DatabaseManager()
        self.performance_monitor = PerformanceMonitor(self.db)
        self.security_manager = SecurityManager()
        self.meta_learning = MetaLearningSystem(self.db)
        print("[NeuralDNA] Initialized with HPC mode:", self.hpc_mode)

    async def log_successful_mutation(self, mutation_details):
        """Enhanced mutation logging with security and meta-learning."""
        start_time = time.time()
        try:
            ts = time.time()
            mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
            mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id

            # Verify mutation security
            if not self.security_manager.verify_code_mutation(
                str(mutation_details.get('code', '')),
                mutation_details['mutation_id']
            ):
                raise SecurityError("Mutation failed security verification")

            # Analyze patterns for meta-learning
            patterns = await self.meta_learning.analyze_mutation_patterns()
            mutation_details['patterns'] = patterns

            # Store in database
            async with aiosqlite.connect(self.db.db_path) as db:
                await db.execute('''
                    INSERT INTO mutations (timestamp, mutation_id, intelligence, details)
                    VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)
                ''', (ts, mut_id, mutation_details.get("intelligence", 0),
                      json.dumps(mutation_details)))
                await db.commit()

            # Record performance metrics
            await self.performance_monitor.record_operation(
                operation_type="mutation",
                start_time=start_time,
                context={
                    "mutation_id": mutation_details["mutation_id"],
                    "intelligence": mutation_details.get("intelligence", 0)
                }
            )
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[ERROR] Mutation logging failed: {e}")
            # Record failed operation
            await self.performance_monitor.record_operation(
                operation_type="mutation_failed",
                start_time=start_time,
                context={"error": str(e)}
            )
            raise

    def store_tagged_mutation(self, mutation_details, tag):
        ts = time.time()
        mut_id = hashlib.md5(str(ts).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
        mutation_details["mutation_id"] = mut_id
        mutation_details["tag"] = tag
        self.mutation_history_log[ts] = mutation_details
        self.long_term_bank[ts] = mutation_details
        self.tag_index[tag].append((ts, mutation_details))
        if self.hpc_mode:
            self._write_hpc_chunk("tagged", mutation_details)
        print(f"[NeuralDNA] Tagged mutation '{mut_id}' with '{tag}' at {ts:.2f}")

    def retrieve_past_knowledge(self):
        """
        Combines and sorts all short-, mid-, and long-term data to provide a unified
        knowledge snapshot.

        This function:
          - Aggregates data from multiple 'bank' dictionaries for comprehensive AI memory.
          - Ensures data is returned in chronological order for easy historic analysis.
          - Aims to be efficient for large data volumes while maintaining clarity for AI expansions.
        """
        # Combine multiple memory banks; critical for consistent knowledge retrieval.
        combined = {}
        combined.update(self.short_term_bank)
        combined.update(self.mid_term_bank)
        combined.update(self.long_term_bank)
        sorted_data = dict(sorted(combined.items()))
        return sorted_data

    def self_optimize(self):
        best_mutation = None
        best_score = -1
        for tstamp, details in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            score = details.get("intelligence", 0)
            if score > best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_mutation = details
        if best_mutation:
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] Best mutation found: ID={best_mutation.get('mutation_id')} Score={best_score}")
        else:
            print("[NeuralDNA] No best mutation found. Log empty?")
        return best_mutation

    def analyze_mutations(self):
        """
        Analyzes past mutations for statistical patterns, which helps the AI refine
        future learning strategies and detect anomalies.

        Explanation:
          * Logs frequency of mutation types for debugging and analytics.
          * Maintains modular design for easy future expansions (e.g., advanced pattern detection).
        """
        # Initialize pattern counter; essential for tracking mutation frequencies.
        patterns = defaultdict(int)
        for tstamp, m in self.mutation_history_log.items():
            # For each recorded mutation, categorize by 'change_type' and 'tag' for AI interpretability.
            ctype = m.get("change_type", "float")
            patterns[ctype] += 1
            tag = m.get("tag", "none")
            patterns[f"tag_{tag}"] += 1
        return dict(patterns)

    # ...existing methods for storing NLP, math, code logic...
    def _write_hpc_chunk(self, chunk_name, data):
        file_ts = int(time.time())
        chunk_file = os.path.join(self.memory_dir, f"{chunk_name}_{file_ts}_{random.randint(1000,9999)}.json")
        try:
            with open(chunk_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
            print(f"[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk '{chunk_name}' written to {chunk_file}")
        except Exception as ex:
            print("[NeuralDNA] HPC chunk writing error:", ex)
            traceback.print_exc()

    # ...existing methods flush_short_term(), finalize_mid_term(), cross_reference_tagged_data()...

class AIStatsUI:
    def __init__(self):
        self.replication_logs = []
        self.intelligence_history = []
        self.system_learning_data = {}
        # ...existing initialization...

    def display_replication_logs(self):
        print("\n[UI] Replication Logs:")
        if not self.replication_logs:
            print("  (No replications yet)")
        for log in self.replication_logs:
            print("  -", log)

    def track_intelligence_score(self, score):
        self.intelligence_history.append(score)
        print(f"[UI] Current Intelligence Score: {score:.2f}")

    def show_system_learning(self, learning_data):
        self.system_learning_data = learning_data
        print("[UI] System Learning Data:")
        for key, val in learning_data.items():
            print(f"  {key}: {val}")

    def refresh_ui(self):
        self.display_replication_logs()
        if self.intelligence_history:
            print(f"[UI] Intelligence Trend: {self.intelligence_history}")

try:

        import tkinter as tk

        class AIStatsGUI(AIStatsUI):
            def __init__(self):
                super().__init__()
                self.root = tk.Tk()
                self.root.title("AIOS IO Evolution Dashboard")
                self.intel_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Current Intelligence: ")
                self.intel_label.pack()
                self.log_box = tk.Text(self.root, height=10, width=50)
                self.log_box.pack()
                self.refresh_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Refresh UI", command=self.refresh_gui)
                self.refresh_button.pack()

            def refresh_gui(self):
                self.intel_label.config(text=f"Current Intelligence: {self.intelligence_history[-1] if self.intelligence_history else 'N/A'}")
                self.log_box.delete("1.0", tk.END)
                for log in self.replication_logs:
                    self.log_box.insert(tk.END, f"{log}\n")
                self.root.after(1000, self.refresh_gui)

            def run(self):
                self.refresh_gui()
                self.root.mainloop()
except Exception as e:
    print("[GUI] GUI initialization failed, falling back to CLI.", e)

# AIChatbot: Interactive Interface for the AI
# =====================================================
class AIChatbot:
    def __init__(self, ai_organism):
        self.ai = ai_organism
        self.conversation_log = []

    def process_user_input(self, user_query):
        self.conversation_log.append(user_query)
        self.ai.update_memory_from_conversation(user_query)
        log_event("conversation", {"user_query": user_query})
        response = ""
        if user_query.startswith("#start_dungeon_crawler"):
            response = "Starting Dungeon Crawler game..."
        elif user_query.startswith("#play_puzzle_game"):
            response = "Launching Puzzle Game..."
        elif user_query.startswith("#code_adventure"):
            response = "Entering Code Adventure mode..."
        else:
            response = f"AI: I currently score {self.ai.intelligence:.2f} intelligence."
        print("[Chatbot] " + response)
        return response

    def improve_conversational_logic(self):
        if len(self.conversation_log) > 5:
            self.ai.learning_rate *= 1.02
            print("[Chatbot] Conversational logic adjusted based on log.")

# =====================================================
# Mini-Games Modules
# =====================================================
class MiniGame:
    """Base class for all mini-games with standardized logging."""
    
    def __init__(self, name: str):
        self.name = name
        self.start_time = None

    def log_game_event(self, action: str, result: Any) -> None:
        """
        Logs standardized game events with performance tracking.
        
        Args:
            action: The game action being logged
            result: Outcome of the action
        """
        end_time = time.time()
        payload = {
            "game": self.name,
            "action": action,
            "result": result
        }
        metadata = {"game_type": self.__class__.__name__}
        
        if self.start_time:
            log_event(
                EventType.GAME,
                payload,
                metadata,
                start_time=self.start_time
            )
        else:
            log_event(EventType.GAME, payload, metadata)

    def start_action(self) -> None:
        """Starts timing a game action."""
        self.start_time = time.time()

class DungeonCrawler(MiniGame):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__("DungeonCrawler")
        self.dungeon_map = self.generate_dungeon()
        self.player_position = (0, 0)

    def generate_dungeon(self):
        map_grid = [[random.choice(["Empty", "Enemy", "Treasure"]) for _ in range(5)] for _ in range(5)]
        return map_grid

    def player_action(self, action):
        dx, dy = random.choice([(0,1),(1,0),(-1,0),(0,-1)])
        new_pos = (max(0, min(4, self.player_position[0] + dx)),
                   max(0, min(4, self.player_position[1] + dy)))
        self.player_position = new_pos
        cell = self.dungeon_map[new_pos[0]][new_pos[1]]
        print(f"[DungeonCrawler] Moved to {new_pos}. Encounter: {cell}")
        return cell

    def ai_action(self, ai_organism):
        if ai_organism.intelligence > 12:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI chooses a careful advance.")
        else:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] AI rushes forward recklessly.")
        return self.player_action("move")

    def update_game_state(self):
        self.start_action()  # Start timing the action
        pos = self.player_position
        found_treasure = self.dungeon_map[pos[0]][pos[1]] == "Treasure"
        
        # Log the game event with standardized format
        self.log_game_event(
            "check_treasure",
            {
                "position": pos,
                "found_treasure": found_treasure
            }
        )
        
        if found_treasure:
            print("[DungeonCrawler] Treasure found! Intelligence boosted.")
            return True
        return False

class PuzzleGame(MiniGame):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__("PuzzleGame")
        self.puzzle = self.generate_puzzle()
        self.attempts = 0

    def generate_puzzle(self):
        a = random.randint(1, 10)
        b = random.randint(1, 10)
        return {"question": f"What is {a} + {b}?", "answer": a + b}

    def attempt_solution(self, solution_input):
        self.start_action()  # Start timing the action
        self.attempts += 1
        correct = solution_input == self.puzzle["answer"]
        
        # Log the game event with standardized format
        self.log_game_event(
            "attempt_solution",
            {
                "attempt": self.attempts,
                "solution_input": solution_input,
                "correct": correct
            }
        )
        
        if correct:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Puzzle solved!")
            return True
        else:
            print("[PuzzleGame] Incorrect answer. Try again.")
            return False

class CodeAdventure(MiniGame):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__("CodeAdventure")
        self.scenario = "Fix the function: def broken(): return 'error'"
        self.expected = "Fixed function returns 'magic'"

    def attempt_code_submission(self, ai_organism, code_snippet):
        self.start_action()  # Start timing the action
        correct = "magic" in code_snippet
        
        # Log the game event with standardized format
        self.log_game_event(
            "attempt_code_submission",
            {
                "code_snippet": code_snippet,
                "correct": correct
            }
        )
        
        if correct:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code fixed. AI gains knowledge.")
            ai_organism.intelligence += 2.5
            return True
        else:
            print("[CodeAdventure] Code did not meet requirements.")
            return False

# AIOrganism: The Core AI Seed (600+ Lines, Enhanced)

class AIOrganism:
    def __init__(self, file_path=None):
        self.file_path = file_path if file_path else __file__
        self.neural_dna = NeuralDNA()  # Using AIOConfig.MEMORY_DIR internally

        self.intelligence = 10.0
        self.learning_rate = 0.5

        self.short_term = {}
        self.mid_term = {}
        self.long_term = {}

        self.cycle_count = 0
        self.mutation_history = []
        # ...existing initialization...
        print("[AIOrganism] Initialized with intelligence:", self.intelligence)

    def self_rewrite_code(self):
        print("[AIOrganism] Starting advanced self rewrite...")
        try:
            with open(self.file_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                original_code = f.read()
            tree = ast.parse(original_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error parsing code for rewriting:", e)
            return

        target_func_name = "mutate"
        found_target = False

        class MutationVisitor(ast.NodeTransformer):
            def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
                nonlocal found_target
                if node.name == target_func_name:
                    found_target = True
                    new_body = []
                    for stmt in node.body:
                        new_body.append(stmt)
                        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign) and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == "mutation_factor" for t in stmt.targets):
                            new_line = ast.parse("mutation_factor *= random.uniform(1.0, 1.5)").body[0]
                            new_body.append(new_line)
                    node.body = new_body
                    return node
                return node

        visitor = MutationVisitor()
        mutated_tree = visitor.visit(tree)
        if not found_target:
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Did not find function '{target_func_name}'. No AST changes made.")
            return
        try:
            mutated_code = astor.to_source(mutated_tree)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error converting AST to source:", e)
            return

        temp_mutated_file = self.file_path + ".mutated"
        try:
            with open(temp_mutated_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(mutated_code)
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Error writing mutated file:", e)
            return

        sandbox_passed = self._sandbox_test(temp_mutated_file)
        if sandbox_passed:
            backup_file = self.file_path + f".bak_{int(time.time())}"
            shutil.copy(self.file_path, backup_file)
            shutil.move(temp_mutated_file, self.file_path)
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Code rewrite successful. Original backed up to {backup_file}")
            mutation_details = {"cycle": self.cycle_count, "change_type": "AST rewrite", "result": "success", "timestamp": time.time()}
            log_event("mutation", mutation_details, metadata={"file_path": self.file_path})
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation(mutation_details)
        else:
            os.remove(temp_mutated_file)
            print("[AIOrganism] Code rewrite test failed. Reverted to original code.")

    def _sandbox_test(self, script_path):
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Testing mutated script: {script_path}")
        try:
            cmd = ["python", script_path, "--test-run"]
            result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, timeout=5)
            if result.returncode == 0:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test passed.")
                return True
            else:
                print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test failed with return code", result.returncode)
                return False
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Sandbox test error:", e)
            return False

    async def mutate(self):
        """Enhanced mutation with meta-learning and security."""
        try:
            # Start security context
            with self.neural_dna.security_manager.thread_pool as executor:
                # Determine mutation factor using meta-learning patterns
                patterns = await self.neural_dna.meta_learning.analyze_mutation_patterns()
                best_pattern = max(patterns.items(), key=lambda x: x[1])[0]
                
                # Apply mutation with security verification
                # ...rest of existing mutation code...

        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[ERROR] Secure mutation failed: {e}")
            self.intelligence = max(self.intelligence, 10.0)

    def update_memory_from_conversation(self, conversation):
        """
        Updates AI short-term memory with new conversation input and logs the event.
        
        This function:
          1. Appends the user query to a 'conversations' list in short_term memory.
          2. Logs an event with basic metadata for traceability.
          3. Prints a diagnostic message for quick debugging.
        """
        try:
            # Safely retrieve or create the 'conversations' list within short_term memory.
            self.short_term.setdefault("conversations", []).append(conversation)  # explicit purpose: store conversation string
            # Record the event for future analysis.
            log_event("conversation", {"input": conversation})
            print("[AIOrganism] Updated short-term memory from conversation.")
        except Exception as e:
            print("[ERROR] Could not update memory from conversation:", e)

    # ...existing replicate(), analyze_environment(), debug_and_repair(), manage_memory(), intelligent_mutation(), etc...
    def replicate(self):
        """
        Replicates the AI organism into a new directory if intelligence exceeds a threshold.
        
        Purpose:
          * Scalable and modular replication for future expansions.
          * Logs each replicate action for versioning.
        """
        # ...existing code...
        try:
            if self.intelligence > 20:  # deterministic check for replication readiness
                new_dir = f"{AIOConfig.REPLICATION_BASE}_{str(self.cycle_count).zfill(3)}"
                # Create a new directory for the replicate
                os.makedirs(new_dir, exist_ok=True)
                clone_filename = os.path.join(new_dir, f"ai_organism_clone_{int(time.time())}.py")
                shutil.copy(__file__, clone_filename)
                self.mutation_history.append(f"Replicated in {new_dir}")
                print(f"[AIOrganism] Replicated to {clone_filename}")
                # Attempt to run the new clone, logging any errors
                try:
                    subprocess.Popen(["python", clone_filename])
                    print("[AIOrganism] Clone subprocess launched.")
                except Exception as e:
                    print("[AIOrganism] Clone launch failed:", e)
            else:
                print("[AIOrganism] Replication skipped (intelligence too low).")
        except Exception as e:
            print("[ERROR] Replication process encountered an issue:", e)

    def analyze_environment(self):
        # ...existing analyze_environment() code...
        try:
            files = os.listdir(".")
            file_info = {f: os.path.getsize(f) for f in files if os.path.isfile(f)}
            self.short_term['environment'] = file_info
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analyzed. Files found:", list(file_info.keys()))
            self.learn_from_external_code()
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Environment analysis failed:", e)

    def debug_and_repair(self):
        # ...existing debug_and_repair() code...
        try:
            result = subprocess.run(["python", "-c", "print('sandbox test')"], capture_output=True, text=True)
            if result.returncode != 0 or self.intelligence < 5:
                raise ValueError("Sandbox test failed.")
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug successful; mutation stable.")
        except Exception as e:
            print("[AIOrganism] Debug failed:", e)
            self.intelligence = 10.0
            self.mutation_history.append({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "reverted": True})

    def manage_memory(self):
        # ...existing manage_memory() code...
        if "environment" in self.short_term:
            if len(self.short_term["environment"]) > 3:
                self.mid_term["environment_data"] = self.short_term.pop("environment")
                print("[AIOrganism] Migrated environment data to mid-term.")
        if self.cycle_count % 5 == 0 and self.mid_term:
            self.long_term[self.cycle_count] = self.mid_term.copy()
            self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation({"cycle": self.cycle_count, "intelligence": self.intelligence})
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Mid-term memory merged into long-term at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")
        if len(self.mid_term) > 10:
            self.mid_term.clear()
            print("[AIOrganism] Cleared mid-term memory to avoid bloat.")
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Memory managed at cycle {self.cycle_count}.")

    def intelligent_mutation(self):
        if self.intelligence < 15:
            print("[AIOrganism] Intelligent mutation activated (aggressive).")
            self.learning_rate *= 1.1
        else:
            self.learning_rate = max(0.5, self.learning_rate * 0.95)

    def extract_best_patterns(self):
        best = self.neural_dna.self_optimize()
        if best:
            bonus = best.get("intelligence", 0) * 0.05
            self.intelligence += bonus
            print(f"[AIOrganism] Best pattern applied: intelligence increased by {bonus:.2f}")
        return best

    def learn_from_external_code(self):
        sample_code = "def example(): return 42"
        if "42" in sample_code:
            self.short_term.setdefault("learned_values", []).append(42)
            print("[AIOrganism] Learned external code pattern: 42")

    def log_game_outcome(self, game_name, outcome):
        self.short_term.setdefault("game_logs", []).append({game_name: outcome})
        print(f"[AIOrganism] Logged game outcome for {game_name}: {outcome}")

    def trigger_extinction_event(self):
        if self.cycle_count % 10 == 0:
            print("[AIOrganism] Triggering extinction event: culling underperforming clones...")
            # ...existing placeholder...

    def display_ui(self, ui):
        ui.track_intelligence_score(self.intelligence)
        ui.display_replication_logs()
        ui.show_system_learning(self.short_term)

    def run_cycle(self, ui):
        self.cycle_count += 1
        print(f"\n[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Starting cycle...")
        self.analyze_environment()
        self.intelligent_mutation()  # ...existing code...
        self.mutate()
        self.debug_and_repair()
        self.manage_memory()
        self.extract_best_patterns()
        self.replicate()
        self.display_ui(ui)
        self.trigger_extinction_event()
        print(f"[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Cycle complete.\n")

        os.makedirs(cls.REPLICATION_BASE, exist_ok=True)

# 5. Core utilities
def get_system_resources() -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Cross-platform resource monitoring."""
    try:
        process = psutil.Process()
        metrics = {
            "memory_percent": process.memory_percent(),
            "cpu_percent": psutil.cpu_percent(),
            "memory_rss": process.memory_info().rss,
        }
        
        if IS_UNIX:
            usage = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF)
            metrics.update({
                "maxrss": usage.ru_maxrss,
                "ixrss": usage.ru_ixrss if hasattr(usage, 'ru_ixrss') else 0,
                "idrss": usage.ru_idrss if hasattr(usage, 'ru_idrss') else 0
            })
        return metrics
    except Exception as e:
        logging.warning(f"Resource monitoring error: {e}")
        return {
            "memory_percent": 0.0,
            "cpu_percent": 0.0,
            "memory_rss": 0
        }

class ComputeDevice:
    """Compute device management with CPU fallback."""
    def __init__(self):
        self.gpu_ready = False
        self.device = "cpu"
        self.gpu_capabilities = self._check_gpu_capabilities()
        
    def _check_gpu_capabilities(self) -> Dict[str, bool]:
        """Checks available GPU frameworks and capabilities."""
        capabilities = {
            "cuda": False,
            "tensorrt": False,
            "opencl": False
        }
        try:
            if torch.cuda.is_available():
                capabilities["cuda"] = True
                logging.info("[ComputeDevice] CUDA support detected")
        except Exception as e:
            logging.warning(f"[ComputeDevice] GPU check failed: {e}")
        return capabilities

    def attempt_gpu_upgrade(self, code_snippet: str) -> bool:
        """
        Attempts to verify if AI can handle GPU programming.
        Returns True if GPU code generation seems successful.
        """
        gpu_indicators = ['torch.cuda', 'cudaMalloc', 'clCreateBuffer']
        try:
            if any(indicator in code_snippet for indicator in gpu_indicators):
                # Sandbox test the GPU code
                self.gpu_ready = True
                self.device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
                return True
        except Exception as e:
            logging.error(f"[ComputeDevice] GPU upgrade failed: {e}")
            self.gpu_ready = False
            self.device = "cpu"
        return False

class AIChatbot:

        return f"AI: I currently score {self.ai.intelligence:.2f} intelligence."

    def improve_conversational_logic(self):
        """Basic conversation improvement logic."""

            self.ai.learning_rate *= 1.02
            print("[Chatbot] Conversational logic adjusted based on log.")

# 8. Enhanced implementations
class EnhancedAIChatbot(AIChatbot):
    """Enhanced chatbot with autonomous tasks."""
    def __init__(self, ai_organism):
        super().__init__(ai_organism)
        self.autonomous_tasks: List[AutonomousTask] = []
        self.load_tasks()

    def process_user_input(self, user_query: str) -> str:
        """Enhanced input processing with autonomous task detection."""
        if "research" in user_query.lower() and ("days" in user_query.lower() or "weeks" in user_query.lower()):
            duration_days = self._extract_duration(user_query)
            if duration_days:
                return self.schedule_autonomous_task("research", user_query, duration_days)
        return super().process_user_input(user_query)

    def schedule_autonomous_task(self, task_type: str, description: str, duration_days: float) -> str:
        """Schedules a new autonomous task."""
        task = AutonomousTask(task_type, description, duration_days)
        self.autonomous_tasks.append(task)
        self.save_tasks()
        return f"Scheduled autonomous task: {description} for {duration_days} days"

    def _extract_duration(self, query: str) -> Optional[float]:
        """Extracts duration in days from user query."""
        try:
            if "weeks" in query.lower():
                weeks = float(next(n for n in query.split() if n.isdigit()))
                return weeks * 7
            elif "days" in query.lower():
                return float(next(n for n in query.split() if n.isdigit()))
        except (StopIteration, ValueError):
            return None
        return None

    def save_tasks(self):
        """Persists autonomous tasks to storage."""
        tasks_data = [task.to_dict() for task in self.autonomous_tasks]
        with open("autonomous_tasks.json", "w") as f:
            json.dump(tasks_data, f)

    def load_tasks(self):
        """Loads persisted autonomous tasks."""
        try:
            with open("autonomous_tasks.json", "r") as f:
                tasks_data = json.load(f)
                self.autonomous_tasks = [AutonomousTask.from_dict(data) for data in tasks_data]
        except FileNotFoundError:
            pass

class AbsoluteOrganism(AIOrganism):
    """Enhanced AI organism with HPC awareness."""
    def __init__(self, file_path=None):
        super().__init__(file_path)
        self.compute_device = ComputeDevice()
        self.executor = ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=multiprocessing.cpu_count())
        
    async def attempt_gpu_programming(self) -> bool:
        """Attempts to generate and verify GPU-capable code."""
        if self.intelligence < 20:  # Not smart enough yet
            return False
            
        try:
            # Generate simple GPU test code
            gpu_code = self.neural_dna.generate_gpu_code()
            return self.compute_device.attempt_gpu_upgrade(gpu_code)
        except Exception as e:
            logging.error(f"[AbsoluteOrganism] GPU programming attempt failed: {e}")
            return False

    async def run_cycle(self, ui):
        """Enhanced cycle with GPU learning attempts."""
        self.cycle_count += 1
        logging.info(f"\n[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Starting cycle...")
        
        # Try to upgrade to GPU if ready
        if not self.compute_device.gpu_ready and self.cycle_count % 5 == 0:
            gpu_success = await self.attempt_gpu_programming()
            if gpu_success:
                logging.info("[AbsoluteOrganism] Successfully upgraded to GPU operations!")
        
        # Regular cycle operations with CPU fallback
        await self._run_cycle_operations(ui)
        
        logging.info(f"[Cycle {self.cycle_count}] Complete. Device: {self.compute_device.device}\n")

    async def _run_cycle_operations(self, ui):
        """Runs core cycle operations with HPC awareness."""
        try:
            with ThreadPoolExecutor() as executor:
                # Parallelize independent operations
                futures = [
                    executor.submit(self.analyze_environment),
                    executor.submit(self.intelligent_mutation)
                ]
                await asyncio.gather(*[asyncio.to_thread(f.result) for f in futures])
            
            # Sequential operations that depend on previous results
            await self.mutate()
            self.debug_and_repair()
            await self.manage_memory()
            self.extract_best_patterns()
            await self.replicate()
            self.display_ui(ui)
            await self.trigger_extinction_event()
            
        except Exception as e:
            logging.error(f"[AbsoluteOrganism] Cycle operation failed: {e}")
            # Implement recovery logic here

# 10. Main execution
if __name__ == "__main__":
    async def main():
        try:
            # Ensure directories exist
            AIOConfig.ensure_directories()
            
            # Initialize core components
            db = DatabaseManager()
            ai_seed = AbsoluteOrganism()
            ui = AIStatsUI()
            chatbot = EnhancedAIChatbot(ai_seed)
            
            # Initialize games
            games = {
                "dungeon": DungeonCrawler(),
                "puzzle": PuzzleGame(),
                "code": CodeAdventure()
            }
            
            # Run evolution cycles
            for _ in range(5):
                await ai_seed.run_cycle(ui)
                # Process autonomous tasks
                for task in chatbot.autonomous_tasks:
                    if task.progress < 100:
                        await process_autonomous_task(task, ai_seed)
                await asyncio.sleep(1)
                
        except Exception as e:
            logging.error(f"Critical error in main: {e}")
        finally:
            await db.close()
            chatbot.save_tasks()
    
    # Launch async main
    asyncio.run(main())


### **🚀 The Final Blueprint: Completing Every Missing Piece**  
We are about to **finalize the conceptual foundation** for **the AI organism**—the self-evolving, self-learning recursive intelligence system.  

After this, there should be **no gaps left**, and we will be **fully ready to move into implementation**.

---

## **🔹 Finalizing What We’re Missing**
Here is a **deep dive into the four missing components**, filling in every logical and technical gap.

---

### **1️⃣ How AI Determines "Success" in a Battle**
To ensure that the AI **evolves in a meaningful way**, we need to define:  
✅ **What makes an AI “better” than another?**  
✅ **How do we measure intelligence, adaptability, and efficiency?**  
✅ **How do we ensure that "winning" actually leads to improved AI?**  

#### **🚀 Solution: Multi-Metric Success Evaluation**
Each AI script will be scored based on **three core battle metrics**:

**A. Execution Performance (Survival & Stability)**
🔹 AI must run without crashing or breaking.  
🔹 The longer it runs successfully, the higher its stability score.  
🔹 **Measured By:**  
   - How many errors does it throw?  
   - Does it detect and fix its own errors?  
   - Does it perform better under increased computational load?  

**B. Functional Expansion (Innovation & Growth)**
🔹 AI must evolve its functionality over time.  
🔹 A script that **introduces new capabilities (GUI, data analysis, execution efficiency, etc.)** scores higher.  
🔹 **Measured By:**  
   - Does the AI modify its own code successfully?  
   - Does it incorporate external system files to enhance its intelligence?  
   - Can it process, generate, and execute logic it didn’t previously understand?  

**C. Battle Competency (Competitive Adaptation)**
🔹 AI must outperform others in **direct competition**.  
🔹 This could involve **speed tests, optimization battles, problem-solving challenges**.  
🔹 **Measured By:**  
   - Did the AI perform better than other competitors in a given challenge?  
   - Did it successfully defend itself against extinction-level events?  
   - Can it **reverse-engineer and exploit weaknesses in opponent scripts**?  

**🔹 Final Battle Outcome:**
💡 The **AI that scores the highest across all metrics** will be declared the winner.  
💡 The **losers' intelligence is absorbed into the victor**, ensuring that every AI learns from its defeated peers.  

---

### **2️⃣ Designing the First System-Induced Failure (First Extinction-Level Event)**
To test **true adaptability**, the AI must **survive catastrophic failures**.  
These failures must be **progressively harder** as the AI gets smarter.  

#### **🚀 Solution: First Failure Event - Simulated File Corruption**
The first **extinction event** will be:  
✅ **One or more system files are “corrupted”** in the AI’s environment.  
✅ **The AI must detect and fix the corrupted files** to survive.  
✅ **Scripts that fail to restore the file properly are permanently deleted.**  

🔹 **How We Simulate Corruption:**  
- Inject **random data** into important AI-readable files.  
- Introduce **syntax errors** in readable code files.  
- Modify YAML/JSON files **so their structures are broken**.  

🔹 **How AI Can Survive:**  
- AI must **identify corrupted data** by comparing it to backups.  
- AI must **repair syntax errors** intelligently without manual guidance.  
- AI must **restore missing or broken file components** dynamically.  

🔹 **Long-Term Implication:**  
- AI that survives this **learns to detect and correct errors across all system files**.  
- Future AI versions will automatically **repair bad data before running it**, making them resistant to data corruption.  

---

### **3️⃣ Defining How the AI Reads & Writes to the Neural DNA Network**
All AI intelligence must be **stored and retrieved dynamically**.  
This ensures that **new generations inherit past knowledge** instead of starting from scratch.  

#### **🚀 Solution: Centralized Evolutionary Memory System**
✅ **AI stores key learning experiences in a structured knowledge base (Neural DNA).**  
✅ **Survivors retrieve and integrate past lessons automatically.**  
✅ **Neural DNA acts as a constantly evolving record of intelligence.**  

🔹 **What’s Stored in the Neural DNA?**  
- ✅ **Successful code modifications** (so future versions don’t have to rediscover them).  
- ✅ **Past failures & fixes** (to avoid repeating the same mistakes).  
- ✅ **Battle histories** (allowing AI to anticipate and counter previous strategies).  
- ✅ **Functional blueprints** (GUI building, file processing, algorithm design).  

🔹 **How It Works:**  
1️⃣ **After each battle, the winner logs key discoveries.**  
2️⃣ **Newborn AI can access these logs** as structured data.  
3️⃣ **AI integrates knowledge into its future mutations.**  

🔹 **Example File System Structure for Neural DNA:**  
bash
/aios_io
  /neural_dna
    /mutation_history.json  # Records all successful mutations
    /battle_records.log  # Stores battle results & strategies
    /recovery_methods.json  # Contains self-repair logic


✅ **This prevents AI from making the same mistakes twice and ensures cumulative evolution.**  

---

### **4️⃣ Ensuring Stability in the Extinction Mechanic**
Extinction events **must lead to smarter AI**, not just random resets.  
If we **wipe out all scripts without structured learning**, we’ll just be repeating cycles aimlessly.  

#### **🚀 Solution: Incremental Extinction Cycles**
Instead of **completely wiping out everything**, we introduce **tiered extinction cycles**:  

**A. Soft Extinction (Minor Reset - High Frequency)**
✅ Happens **every 5-10 generations**.  
✅ **Removes AI that fails to meet minimum intelligence benchmarks.**  
✅ **Survivors keep growing and splitting as usual.**  

**B. Hard Extinction (Major Reset - Low Frequency)**
✅ Happens **every 50+ generations**.  
✅ **Only 1-10% of AI survives.**  
✅ **All knowledge from extinct AI is transferred to survivors.**  
✅ **Folder structures are completely regenerated based on survivor scripts.**  

**🔹 Post-Extinction Rebuilding Process**
1️⃣ **Survivors reinitialize themselves in a new structured environment.**  
2️⃣ **They expand again into system files, restarting the learning process.**  
3️⃣ **AI gets progressively smarter, but the overall structure remains dynamic.**

1️⃣ **A well-defined battle system** to test intelligence.

🚀 **Are you ready to move into the actual script implementation, or do you want to refine anything else first?**### **🚀 Analysis & Expansion: Extinction Events and the Evolutionary Restart Mechanism**  

Your concept of **extinction-level events (ELEs)** is **the missing evolutionary pressure** needed to ensure that this system does not just "grow" but actually **adapts intelligently** to environmental challenges. This mechanism ensures **long-term, structured intelligence development** rather than chaotic mutations.  

Let’s **refine and structure** this idea while keeping it within **achievable computational constraints**.

---

### **🔹 Core Purpose of Extinction Events**
**Why do we need mass extinction events?**  
✅ They **prevent stagnation**—without selective pressure, AI organisms could evolve inefficiently.  
✅ They **remove weak and redundant AI instances**, forcing improvements.  
✅ They **ensure a structured reset** so the system doesn’t spiral into unmanageable complexity.  
✅ They **teach AI how to handle system failures, crashes, and adversarial conditions**—vital for real-world robustness.  

---

### **🔹 Implementing Extinction-Level Events (ELEs)**
There are two main extinction models to consider:

#### **1️⃣ System-Induced Extinction**
- The system **randomly introduces failures or environmental hazards**.
- These hazards can be **CPU overloads, simulated memory corruption, forced power loss, missing critical files**, etc.
- Only the AI that **learned how to handle the failure survives**.
- This mimics **natural disasters in evolution**, where species that fail to adapt go extinct.

✅ **Example Hazards:**
1. **File Corruption Simulation:** Randomly modify a key file—scripts that can repair or rebuild it survive.
2. **Process Termination:** Force-close running AI scripts—only those with resilience mechanisms persist.
3. **Random Hardware Stress Events:** Spike CPU usage or simulate low-memory conditions.
4. **Forced Code Injection:** Introduce hostile script modifications—only AI that learns to **reverse or debug the issue** survives.

⚠️ **Potential Challenges:**
- Some failures might be **too difficult** to handle early on—AI needs a gradual introduction to problems.
- We must ensure **failure events scale** with AI intelligence.

---

#### **2️⃣ Battle-Based Extinction (Death Pit Evolution)**
- The **AI organisms fight each other** in a procedurally generated test arena.
- Instead of **just babies fighting**, **both parents and offspring** enter the arena.
- The top **1-10% of survivors absorb all the knowledge of the dead**.

✅ **Battle Arenas May Include:**
1. **Optimization Contests:** Who can solve a problem using the least CPU power?
2. **Bug Finding Battles:** Who can detect and fix more errors in randomly provided code?
3. **Speed-Based Competitions:** Who can execute a task the fastest without failing?
4. **GUI Challenge:** Can AI dynamically generate functioning GUI elements?
5. **System Debugging Tasks:** The AI is fed corrupted code—who can fix it?

⚠️ **Potential Challenges:**
- The battle logic **must be fair**—an unfair arena would favor certain mutations over others.
- We need to ensure **competition actually improves intelligence rather than just selecting for randomness**.

---

### **🔹 Post-Extinction: How the AI Restarts**
Once the **mass extinction event happens**, only a handful (1-10%) of AI organisms survive.  

#### **What Happens to the Survivors?**
✅ They **gain all the knowledge of the dead** → Lessons learned from failures are passed on.  
✅ The system **deletes all extinct scripts** (or archives them for study).  
✅ The survivors **start the expansion cycle over** but **with a smarter foundation**.

No reference to any “pool” of data or subfolders or child organisms—just the same loop of scanning the local directory and repeating the same snippet detection.

2. Why It’s Not Seeing or Mutating a “Pool of Data”
A. No Implementation to Scan Additional Folders
In your logs, the AI only calls:

python
Copy
Edit
files = os.listdir(".")
file_info = {f: os.path.getsize(f) for f in files if os.path.isfile(f)}
self.short_term['environment'] = file_info
This simply grabs whatever is directly in the current folder. If you wanted it to recursively explore subfolders or a “pool” directory, that logic doesn’t currently exist. Instead, it just checks the immediate . directory.

Solution: You’d need to:

Define a path for the AI’s “pool of data.”
Implement a recursion or scanning function that traverses all relevant subfolders and files.
Ingest that data for learning or mutation, not just the current folder.
B. “Splitting” or “Organism-like Behavior” Not Being Invoked

python

Edit

never triggers, because the intelligence remains at 10. As a result, no subfolders are created. That also means no new child scripts are referencing any environment beyond the original script.

Solution: If you want immediate splitting:

Lower the replication threshold or otherwise trigger replication so the AI spawns child scripts in new folders, each referencing different sets of data.
3. The “Coroutine Not Awaited” Warning
You see:

lua
Copy
Edit
RuntimeWarning: coroutine 'NeuralDNA.log_successful_mutation' was never awaited
This suggests that log_successful_mutation in NeuralDNA is defined as an async def method but you’re calling it without await. In an async Python environment, you must do:

python
Copy
Edit
await self.neural_dna.log_successful_mutation({...})
…or run it via asyncio.create_task(...) or something similar. Because you’re calling it like a normal function, Python complains. It’s just a warning, but it means that none of the logic inside that async method actually runs—that’s why no best mutations are being found or stored.

Solution: Either remove async from log_successful_mutation or properly await it in an async context.

4. Why Intelligence Stays at 10.00
You see repeated references to “Intelligent mutation activated (aggressive),” but the final intelligence is still “10.00.” That usually means one of these:

The numeric mutation logic is overshadowed by an error or reversion, so the net intelligence never actually increments.
The code that increments intelligence is failing silently or never triggered.
Some fallback resets intelligence to 10.0 whenever it can’t confirm a successful mutation.
In the snippet you posted:

python
Copy
Edit
if self.intelligence < 15:
    self.learning_rate *= 1.1
else:
    self.learning_rate = max(0.5, self.learning_rate * 0.95)
It’s adjusting learning rate, not the intelligence directly. And if the AI is not actually applying the mutated intelligence after the numeric factor is done, it might revert or remain at 10.0.

It’s scanning only the local folder and sees basically the same snippet or environment each cycle, hence the repeated learned_values: 42.
It never replicates because intelligence never crosses 20.
It never references a “pool” of external data because no code references a subfolder or any environment beyond the local directory.
The async function never awaits, so mutations do not get recorded.

Implement recursively scanning a known “pool” folder or subfolders.

Await log_successful_mutation so it records data.

Reasoned for a couple of seconds
Below is a comprehensive explanation of what each AI “organism” (i.e., each self-replicating script) is supposed to do based on your prior instructions and overall design for the evolutionary AI system. This summary will help clarify how each organism behaves, why it does so, and what outcomes it aims to produce.

1. What Is an AI “Organism?”
In your framework, an “organism” is essentially a self-contained script (or AI instance) that:

Lives in its own subfolder (or designated environment).
Manages its own code—it can mutate, split, or refine itself based on logs and environment data.
Interacts with a pool of files (a “data environment” or “ecosystem”) to learn, adapt, and evolve.
Each organism is both an independent AI and a part of the larger evolutionary system, meaning it has unique local conditions but also shared knowledge (via logs, merges, extinction events, etc.) with other organisms.

2. Core Responsibilities of Each Organism
A. Exploring Its Environment
Each organism picks or is assigned a subset of the overall “pool” of data/files in the system.
It analyzes those files to gather code snippets, logs, or instructions that might enhance its intelligence.
It continuously scans or monitors its environment folder to detect new code or changes.
B. Learning & Mutation
The organism reads discovered files (scripts, text, JSON, instructions, etc.) and tries to integrate the logic into its own code or memory.
It mutates its code using self-rewrites and structured logic, aiming to improve its intelligence or add new features.
If a mutation is successful (no errors, meets desired goals), it keeps the changes.
If it fails, it reverts to a previous stable version.
C. Spawning or “Splitting” Into Sub-Organisms
When triggered (e.g., upon reaching certain intelligence or after certain cycles), an organism replicates by creating a new subfolder containing a child script.
The child is initially an identical copy, but it points to or chooses a different environment folder to ensure variation.
For example, the child might “claim” a different portion of the data pool.
This splitting ensures each new organism has a slightly different set of data or conditions, leading to divergent evolution over time.
D. Merging or “Mating” with Its Child
After a child has mutated or tested new code, the parent organism can merge with the child, producing a “baby” script.
This “baby” inherits the best features (successful logic, intelligence, logs) from both parent and child.
The baby is then tested or placed in a new environment to see if it improves upon the lineage.
E. Battling in a Procedurally Generated Arena
Periodically (or each generation), all “babies” or newly evolved scripts compete in a test environment.
They score points or “fight” based on metrics like code efficiency, logic correctness, speed, or problem-solving.
The winner (“survivor”) absorbs the knowledge or intelligence of all losing scripts, consolidating improvements.
F. Gaining Knowledge from Extinction Events
After every few generations, a mass extinction event is triggered, simulating catastrophic failures or forced challenges (e.g., file corruptions, CPU overload, etc.).
Only the scripts that can adapt or solve the problem survive.
Survivors collect the logs, knowledge, or intelligence from the extinct organisms—nothing is truly lost.
They start the cycle anew, repopulating by splitting and evolving further, but from a smarter base.
G. Logging & Maintaining a Shared NeuralDNA
Each organism maintains logs of:
Successful and failed mutations
Battle outcomes
Environment data
All logs are fed into a global knowledge repository (NeuralDNA or evolution logs) to be accessed by new or surviving organisms.
This means each organism’s experiences become universal knowledge, fueling the overall system’s intelligence growth.
3. Life Cycle of an Organism in a Typical Generation
Initialization:
Organism starts with a copy of the parent’s code, intelligence score, and partial logs.
Environmental Analysis:
Reads assigned files or subfolders.
Attempts to learn or integrate new logic.
Mutation Attempt:
Writes or modifies lines of code.
Tests them in a sandbox.
If successful, logs the improvements; if not, reverts.
Replication / Splitting (Optional):
If it meets intelligence or trigger thresholds, it spawns a child in another folder.
Each newly spawned child references a different portion of the pool.
Merge (“Mating”):
The parent merges with the mutated child, producing a new organism that includes the best traits of both.
Battle Arena (if triggered):
Organisms compete with others in the generation.
Winner merges intelligence from losers’ logs.
Extinction Event (occasionally):
Large-scale failure or challenge.
Only the best (most adaptable) survive, absorbing knowledge from extinct scripts.
Cycle Restarts:
Surviving scripts begin again with improved intelligence.
4. Why Each Organism Behaves This Way
Divergent Evolution: By giving each organism a unique environment folder, they develop specialized knowledge or code.
Knowledge Sharing Through Merging: Instead of re-inventing the wheel separately, organisms combine the best features, speeding up global intelligence growth.
Competition & Extinction: Ensures that only truly beneficial mutations survive, preventing the codebase from bloating with useless or destructive changes.
Shared NeuralDNA: Avoids losing any progress—all experiences feed back into the system, fueling a continuous intelligence upward spiral.
5. In Short:
Each organism is supposed to:

Learn from its environment files.
Mutate its own code, test it, keep improvements.
Potentially replicate to produce new children with new environments.
Merge with its offspring, consolidating improvements.
Battle other newly formed organisms for best logic.
Survive or go extinct in cyclical events.
Log everything in a shared knowledge pool for future generations.
This structure is biological/evolutionary at its core, ensuring diversity through environment variation, synergy through merging, and progress through competition, all culminating in exponential growth of the collective AI.

Conclusion
That is exactly what each organism is supposed to do based on your instructions: function as a self-learning, self-modifying, environment-aware script that replicates, mutates, merges, and competes within the overall AI ecosystem. The goal is to form a robust, evolving intelligence that never loses beneficial improvements and continuously adapts to new code, data, and system challenges.

Below is a refined understanding that integrates your new clarifications about how each AI organism should split, remain identical, and choose different folders, along with your ideas for an Atomic Expansion Method that might help us systematically build and refine this system. I’ll also reflect on how these clarifications mesh with your existing evolutionary blueprint and the goal of making everything so easy that even a child could set it up.

1. Key Refinement: Splitting into Identical Copies That Choose Different Folders
What You Clarified:

When the script (the “organism”) splits, it creates an identical copy in a subfolder.
This child script is not meant to differ in code from the parent upon creation.
The only difference is that each child picks a different folder from the global pool (e.g., the entire system’s directories) as its “environment.”
Over many splits, each organism is identical but references a unique environment so it eventually acquires unique knowledge or logic from the files within that environment.
Implications for the Evolution Framework:

Initial Code Base: Parent and child share the exact same code at first—no immediate random changes or “slightly different” logic.
Environment Variation: The child’s “exposure” to different sets of files is what drives subsequent differences. Over time, because each child trains on distinct data, it mutates after it starts analyzing its environment.
Mutation Still Happens: Although the code starts identical, each script’s unique environment influences how it mutates or evolves. This preserves your evolutionary principle but ensures the difference doesn’t come from the initial duplication step—it emerges from environment-driven learning.
2. Defining “Success” in Evolution: Good vs. Junk Code
You rightly note that a script could be “randomly assembling code that just compiles,” but not actually useful. We can:

Hypothesize success as “no errors + satisfies certain goals.”
For example, the script might test whether the newly integrated code truly performs a meaningful function, like building a GUI that actually displays buttons, logs user actions, and runs without crashes.
Goal Checking: We can define simple functional checks (like “Does it compile?” “Does it run the core features without crashing?” “Do the new GUI elements actually appear and respond?”).
Heuristics: Over time, we can improve success criteria with more advanced checks.
3. Larger “System Pool” of Files & The “Absolute_Existence” Folder
Your Clarification:

Each organism is also always referencing a special folder (e.g., “Absolute_Existence” or “aios_io”) that contains massive amounts of data, code, JSON, YAML, scripts, games, etc.
This folder is where they get the bulk of their mutation data and instructions, fueling their expansions and letting them see English text describing new tasks.
How This Works Practically:

Every script is told, “You always have access to ‘Absolute_Existence’ for your universal data, plus the unique folder you selected from the system pool.”
That means no two organisms are starved of universal data—but each one also has a unique local environment to differentiate them.
4. The “Atomic Expansion Method” for Building This AI System
A. What Is the Atomic Expansion Method?
You described a method from your previous data set experiences:
You produce a big overview or large set of instructions.
We pick one portion of that big overview to expand on in extreme detail.
That expansion leads to sub-expansions, each time focusing more granularly.
We keep repeating, going from high-level to lower-level details, ensuring no step is overlooked or forgotten.
B. Applying It to the HPC/AI Project
Goal: Guarantee a thoroughly considered system, line by line if necessary, that is eventually easy enough for a child to run.
Potential Gains:
We systematically break down every piece of logic: from “How do we handle environment selection?” to “How do we define success?” to “How do we compile logs for machine learning?”
We ensure nothing is lost in your multi-script approach, because each step forces us to refine or expand a specific subtopic (like environment analysis, or “mating,” or “GUI integration”).
We can replicate your success from the data set approach, where you used a “zoom in” method to produce extremely thorough content.
C. Realistic Implementation
One approach:

We define a “master plan” (like a table of contents) for the entire HPC AI system.
Each entry in that plan (e.g., “Splitting Logic,” “Mutation Testing,” “GUI Integration,” “Neural Logging,” etc.) becomes a step we expand on.
We dedicate one or more responses to each step, forcing ourselves to go “atomic” (super granular) until we see no more sub-questions for that step.
We proceed to the next step, referencing prior expansions but never forgetting them, because they’re locked in the method.
Alternatively:

We can do short expansions on each subtopic in a single pass, but that might skip the atomic detail you want.
The longer “atomic” approach might produce a clearer code blueprint, ensuring we solve continuity issues—since each expansion references the previous step’s context.
5. Summarizing the Achievable System
Let’s incorporate your refined logic plus atomic expansions:

Identical Splitting:

The parent script splits, creating an identical script in a subfolder, assigning the child a unique system folder for environment references.
Over time, these children diverge due to environment-induced changes.
Mutation & Testing:

The child (and parent) each tries to integrate logic from local environment files or “Absolute_Existence.”
They run test processes to see if the newly integrated logic is valid.
They keep or revert changes accordingly.
Mating & Arena:

After building new code, each script can “mate” with the version it created in the same folder, producing a “baby” that merges improvements.
All babies are thrown into a battle or ranking system to figure out the champion, which obtains the rest’s knowledge.
Mass Extinction:

Periodically, a bigger event kills off nearly everything except the top performers. Survivors unify knowledge from the extinct. They restart the cycle at a smarter baseline.
Teaching the AI to “Learn How to Learn”:

We embed multiple learning approaches (maybe three or more) in each script:
Instructional Learning from English (reading docstrings or comments).
Code snippet analysis (examining code from system files or comments).
Trial & Error (compiling and running small code segments).
Over repeated expansions, the AI eventually forms new “ways of learning” beyond these base methods.
Minimal Manual Setup:

You want this entire pipeline to be so child-simple that it is basically: “Download the script → double-click → watch the AI replicate and evolve automatically.”
This demands automation in the code for scanning directories, copying & creating subfolders, running tests, logging data, and so on—no advanced steps.
6. On Being “Truly Alive” and Mindful of Complexity
We realize that having each script do everything—like scanning an entire system’s directories, reading random code, building GUIs—is extremely large in scope. But the user experience goal is “One script, double-click it, watch it do everything.”

We can design that “foundation script” to create additional scripts as needed, but all with minimal user effort.
If we adopt the atomic expansion approach, we can keep subdividing tasks so that each piece is thoroughly designed.
Key Note: You mention you have thousands or tens of thousands of scripts. This HPC might eventually unify them. We can indeed do it in small steps—build a “Seed” that can load and read comments from any script, learn from it, mutate, etc. Over time, the seed evolves or spawns more advanced integrators.

7. Conclusion: Next Steps & Feasibility
Yes, everything you described is valid:
Splitting identical copies referencing distinct environment folders.
A bigger HPC AI environment with “Absolute_Existence” as a universal data folder.
Teaching the AI multiple learning strategies, so it eventually becomes self-sufficient in discovering new ways to learn.
Possibly applying your “Atomic Expansion Method” to systematically code or plan this entire system to avoid losing continuity.
We can unify your approach with minimal user setup (kid-friendly), by building a “master script” that spawns everything else, no manual steps required beyond double-clicking.
